# MS-2 — Scoring: Design

> Design output (2026-09-06, revision 12 — **owner-ratified 2026-09-06: every one of the 27 §1.2 decisions carries an `accept` disposition, none overridden**). Instantiates master-spec §19 "MS-2 — Domain logic" as five sequenced sub-milestones, each with its own implementation plan and its own `dev→main` PR, the way the [MS-1 ingestion design](2026-07-05-ms1-ingestion-design.md) instantiated §19 "MS-1 — Core workflow". The master spec and ADRs remain the design source of truth; this document records the MS-2 *decomposition* and the scope decisions §1.2 put to the owner, now ratified. It introduces no new architecture except where §1 says so explicitly and identifies it as a decision the owner disposed of — every other mechanism below is fixed by an existing ADR or spec section, cited inline.
>
> **Revision 2 (2026-09-06)** answered cross-agent review round 1 (findings SA-001…SA-010) and an independent code-claim verification pass. Material changes: §1 becomes a ratification table with an explicit owner-disposition column; §2.2 defines one scoring population; §2.3 adopts a conservative eBay compliance boundary and a derived-field clearing contract; §3.2.1 fixes the unit-inconsistent veto formula; §3.2.2 folds lot quantity into `$/TB`; §3.2.3 replaces a mathematically false `n_eff` property; §3.3.1–§3.3.3 make cohort bucketing and relaxation deterministic and total and correct a false claim about where N2 attributes are persisted; §3.4.1–§3.4.3 define a complete invalidation model, a canonical-digest write-on-change rule, and an immutable version-dispatch contract.
>
> **Revision 3 (2026-09-06)** answers cross-agent review round 2 (SA-011…SA-014 new; SA-002, SA-003, SA-004, SA-010 partially resolved). Material changes, in the order the review's own dependency runs: **§3.4.1 removes trigger B7** — the reviewer is right that exponential decay is scale-invariant, so nothing drifts between real dependency changes (SA-014), and the baseline gains a semantic `baseline_digest` plus a `last_evaluated_at` freshness stamp so incidental recomputation identity leaves the decision digest alone; **§3.3.4 (new)** pins `q` as an ascending weighted price rank with half-weight ties and persists the ordered observation vector that makes a listing-only rescore reproduce it exactly (SA-012); **§3.2.4 (new)** gives exhaustive numeric `T` / `W` / `C` / availability lookup tables over the current enums (SA-011, decision S2-17); **§2.1 and §4** replace `RunKind.SCORE` with a dedicated `ScoringRun` model that carries no source attribution (SA-013, decision S2-19); **§2.3(b)** makes expiry clearing conditional on the exact current row identity (SA-002); **§3.4.1** rebases the L2 digest on one canonical dependency document carrying full extracted-attribute provenance and gives B5 a persisted stamp (SA-003); **§3.3.2** defines ad-hoc bucket adjacency and the nearest-bucket tie rule with exact fixtures (SA-004); **§3.4.3** freezes the whole transitive dependency closure of a released version (SA-010). Decisions **S2-17, S2-18, S2-19** are new and pending.
>
> **Revision 4 (2026-09-06)** answers cross-agent review round 3 (SA-015…SA-019 new; SA-010 partially resolved; SA-002…SA-009 and SA-011…SA-014 confirmed resolved and carried forward unchanged). Material changes: **§3.3.5 (new)** makes cohort baselines **append-only versions** with a separate `cohort_baseline_current` pointer, gives every score an immutable baseline FK, states the version retention class and the prune rule, and defines the sweep (SA-015, decision S2-20); **§3.4.1 group 4** now carries the *sufficient* price evidence (`q`, `n_eff`, `λ`, the listing's own `ln_price_per_tb` and `w_rel`, and `baseline_digest`) so a historical score re-derives from `inputs_json` alone (SA-015); **§3.2.5 (new)** gives the seller-feedback provider contract — field semantics, deterministic normalization into `p_obs`/`n`/`y`, malformed and missing behavior, and latest-observation selection — and rewrites `SellerRatingObservation` to match (SA-016, decision S2-21); **§3.3.4** names the **price-event projection** once and splits `raw_snapshot_count` from `price_event_count` so B3 compares like with like (SA-017); **§2.2.1 (new)** defines the deterministic current-offer selector and adds rescore trigger **L8** (SA-018, decision S2-22); **§3.2.6 (new)** pins `is_provisional := n_eff < 30` alone with an enumerated `provisional_reasons` list, and demotes `warranty_unverified` to a pure risk flag (SA-019, decision S2-23); **§3.4.3** brings dispatch, parsing, and the registry inside each version's protected closure and makes retirement an operational procedure against authoritative datasets (SA-010). Decisions **S2-20, S2-21, S2-22, S2-23** are new and pending.
>
> **Revision 5 (2026-09-06)** answers cross-agent review round 4 (SA-020…SA-024 new; SA-010, SA-015 and SA-016 partially resolved; SA-002…SA-009, SA-011…SA-014 and SA-017…SA-019 confirmed resolved and carried forward unchanged). Its theme is **invalidation completeness**: every persisted input now has a named trigger for insert, update, delete/expiry **and** backfill, and §3.4.1's new **Dependency × mutation coverage** table is where that is checked rather than asserted. Material changes, in dependency order: **§2.2.2 (new)** adds `OfferSnapshot.ingested_at`, a server-assigned insertion stamp that no caller may supply, and rebuilds **B1** as an ingestion high-water mark with a stated commit-lag margin, so a backfilled older snapshot can no longer evade invalidation (SA-020, decision S2-24); **§3.3.4** redefines the price-event projection `E` over each listing's **full retained history** with explicit left-censored carry-in, so raw-snapshot window exits inside an unchanged price run stop churning the representative, fixes the weight anchor to the newest *event*, and turns **B3** into a semantic `price_event_digest` instead of a count (SA-021, SA-020, decision S2-25); **§3.4.1** adds **L9** (deletion or expiry of the selected seller rating) with its sweep hook, rebuilds B5 as a **`member_projection_digest`** over each member's cohort-shaping extracted projection, evaluated over the union of stored and live cohort keys so both the old and the new cohort are dirtied (SA-023), and states the refresh's candidate-set derivation once; **§3.4.1 group 4 becomes the "baseline evidence snapshot"** and carries the weighted quartiles, `margin_iqr` and the rendered source-composition facts, so `explanation_json` and `explanation_text` regenerate with no baseline access at all (SA-015); **§3.2.5(d)** selects the latest observation **regardless of usability** (SA-016); **§3.4.3** dispatches a historical version without passing through `compose.py` and defines retirement against **every retained restorable backup** (SA-010); **§3.3.5(c) and MS-2a** make the baseline FK nullable exactly for the enumerated no-baseline `price_basis` values under a CHECK constraint (SA-024, decision S2-26). Decisions **S2-24, S2-25, S2-26** were new in that revision and are ratified with the rest (§1.2).

> **Revision 7 (2026-09-06)** answers cross-agent review round 5 — the audit's **final** round (SA-025, SA-026 and SA-027 new; SA-019 reopened at medium; the other 23 findings confirmed resolved and carried forward unchanged). Material changes: **§2.1, §2.3(a), §3.3.4, §3.3.5(a)–(b) and §3.4.1** move every mutable detection stamp — `next_window_exit_at`, `price_event_digest`, `member_projection_digest`, `raw_snapshot_count` — off the immutable version row onto the `cohort_baseline_current` pointer, where they are rewritten on **every** successful evaluation including a digest-identical one, and fold the **semantic** source composition into `baseline_digest` so explanation-bearing provenance can never be stale on a reused version (SA-025); **§3.3.4** withdraws the midrank-inverse quartile rule, which is not total, for a **total** inverse weighted CDF, proves its relation to the unchanged midrank `q`, and pins singleton, all-equal, two-point and tie-heavy fixtures (SA-026); **§3.2.6, §3.3.5(c), §3 (MS-2a) and §3.4.1 group 4** fix **one** neutral representation — `n_eff = 0`, `λ = 0` as stored zeros, `q` / quartiles / `margin_iqr` NULL — with the matching column nullability (SA-027); and **§1.2's S2-23 row** completes its `provisional_reasons` enumeration with `price_unavailable` (SA-019). **No ratified decision changes meaning**: S2-23's Boolean, S2-18's `q` and observation vector, S2-20's append-only versioning and S2-7's neutral subscore are all unchanged, and every fix lands in the normative section the §1.2 row already points at. The audit reached its **five-round cap** with these four findings closed **after** the last peer round, so revision 7 carries **no peer confirmation** — in-house adversarial verification only (§6, §7).

> **Revision 8 (2026-09-06)** answers round 1 of the **fresh peer audit `b2eedc33-99bf-4c33-bbfb-bb7b2bed7cd3`**, opened against revision 7 because the owner chose peer confirmation over cutting the MS-2a plan unconfirmed (§7). Four findings, all closed here: **§2.1, §2.2.1, §2.3(b) and §3.4.1 candidate clause C9 (new)** make a listing whose §2.2.1 current offer snapshot has moved an explicit, indexed member of the refresh candidate set, so an eBay or same-price stock transition can no longer leave a scored listing stale (SA-001); **§3.3.5(i) (new)**, with §3.3.5(a)–(c), §2.3(a), §3.4.1 B2/B3/L1/C2 and MS-2a, defines the **baseline-void** state a cohort enters when its last usable price event leaves `E` — a NULL `current_version` on a surviving pointer, a total fan-out to the members' own relaxation ladders, and the reverse transition back to a version (SA-002); **§3.3.5(a) and test 2** replace the model-`save()`-only immutability promise with a **database `BEFORE UPDATE` trigger** that no ORM, bulk or raw path bypasses, and make the admin read-only (SA-003); and the **master-spec glossary row for `offer_snapshot`** is corrected to stop naming the score, with the design's §6 hygiene row citing the revision that did it (SA-004). **No ratified decision changes meaning**: S2-20's append-only versioning and absolute no-UPDATE rule are strengthened rather than narrowed, S2-22's selector is unchanged (only its candidate reach is stated), S2-26's CHECK pairing is unchanged and the void state reaches it through the already-enumerated `no_cohort_evidence` value, and S2-1's single score home is what the glossary fix restores. No new §1.2 row is opened — each fix lands in the normative section its existing row already points at, the same treatment revision 7 gave SA-025…SA-027.
>
> **Revision 9 (2026-09-06)** answers round 2 of audit `b2eedc33-99bf-4c33-bbfb-bb7b2bed7cd3`, which confirmed all four round-1 findings resolved and raised two new blocking boundaries. Both are closed here. **§2.2.1, §3.2.4, §3.2.6 and §3.4.1 group 1** define the **complete group-1 payload when `S(listing)` does not exist**: the price facts are JSON `null` under explicit keys and `stock_status` is the literal `StockStatus.UNKNOWN` enum value, so the mandatory availability subscore reads a real cell of §3.2.4's exhaustive table (`s_avail = 0.50`, `stock_unknown`) instead of a row that was never selected — the alternative of a second, stock-only selector is rejected in §2.2.1 with the four mechanisms it would have to duplicate (SA-005). **§3.4.2 (new serialization contract), §2.3(b), §3.4.1 L8 and MS-2a** define the **cross-job protocol between the score refresh and the retention sweep**: the parent `listing` row is the single serialization point, no transaction may delete a listing's `offer_snapshot` rows without holding it, the refresh re-runs the §2.2.1 selector *under* that lock and abandons its write when the selection moved, and the sweep gains one generic `lock_parents_for_delete` hook — with the reason a bare `EXISTS` guard cannot substitute for the lock stated from PostgreSQL's documented READ COMMITTED re-check rule (SA-006). **No ratified decision changes meaning**: S2-22's selector is untouched (revision 9 defines only what happens when it returns nothing), S2-7's neutral subscore, S2-23's Boolean and S2-26's CHECK pairing are unchanged, and S2-4's "no queue" and S2-3's 60 s budget are preserved because the protocol adds locks rather than state. Three in-house verifier corrections ride along in §3 (MS-2a), §3.3.5(a) and §1.2's lead-in.
>
> **Revision 10 (2026-09-06)** answers round 3 of audit `b2eedc33-99bf-4c33-bbfb-bb7b2bed7cd3`, which confirmed both round-2 findings resolved and raised one new blocking finding. **§3.4.2 generalizes the commit-time revalidation contract from the §2.2.1 offer selector to every selected child input of `D`** (SA-007). Revision 9's step (b) re-ran only the offer selector, so a sweep that locked the listings, deleted the selected `SellerRatingObservation`, fired L9 and committed could still be overwritten by a refresh whose offer check passed — and nothing would repair it, because L9's clear is one-time and C5 detects only a *newer* observation. The replacement is one comparison, not one per dependency: **`D_selected`** is the sub-document of `D` produced by a selector over rows the refresh does not lock while computing, it is re-derived under the listing lock and compared as canonical JSON, and any difference abandons the whole write. Its members are enumerated (offer facts; the seller aggregate **including its fallbacks to an unusable row and to the S2-12 prior**), and every other `D` term is stated as covered by something else — the locked row itself, the bounded-class filter, or the baseline FK's `PROTECT` — rather than left unmentioned. The **invariant** is widened with it: what a deleting transaction must lock is the set of listings whose decision the deleted evidence feeds, which for a seller rating is a fan-out and not an FK parent. **§3.4.1 L9 and C5** stop claiming a completeness they never had, and five two-connection barrier fixtures cover both orderings and all three fallback shapes. **No ratified decision changes meaning**: S2-21's seller contract and S2-12's prior are read, not redefined; S2-4's "no queue" and the single listing-first lock order are preserved.
>
> **Revision 11 (2026-09-06)** answers round 4 of audit `b2eedc33-99bf-4c33-bbfb-bb7b2bed7cd3`, which confirmed SA-007 resolved and returned two blocking findings. **§3.2.5(d), §3.4.1 C5, L4 and the coverage table make the seller candidate clause total in the direction it never covered** (SA-008): the group-3 selected `observed_at` is NULL on the S2-12 no-row prior, so C5's bare SQL `>` was unknown rather than true and a seller's **first** genuine rating could leave every already-scored listing on the prior indefinitely. C5 now carries the same `COALESCE` shape C9 uses, the prior → first-row transition is stated normatively for both a usable and an unusable first row, and it is self-clearing in both branches because §3.2.5(d) selects the latest row regardless of usability. **§3.4.2 replaces the fan-out lock with an *anchor* lock** (SA-009): `listings in P for seller S` is a mutable predicate, and a row lock cannot lock a row that does not exist yet, so a listing entering `P` after the sweep selected its fan-out — or reassigned into it — could be scored from a rating whose deletion was already in flight, minting a historical row that outlives the evidence. The anchor is the **`seller` row**, which every path into the fan-out must name; the deleter takes it `FOR UPDATE` and the refresh `FOR SHARE` before selecting the aggregate, so membership stops being part of the correctness argument. **The lock order becomes two classes — `seller` → `listing`, ascending within each — and revisions 9–10's one-directional wait-graph proof is withdrawn rather than patched**, because the refresh now takes two locks; a total class order replaces it. The dependency × mutation pass is re-run with fan-out membership as an input in its own right and its result recorded as a new coverage row. **No ratified decision changes meaning**: S2-21's seller contract, S2-12's prior and S2-16's population predicate are read rather than redefined, and S2-4 holds — an anchor row is a lock, not a queue, and no column, table or schema change is added.
>
> **Revision 12 (2026-09-06, with a same-day wording pass)** answers round 5 of audit `b2eedc33-99bf-4c33-bbfb-bb7b2bed7cd3` — **the audit's final round, which it reached at its five-round cap without converging**. SA-001…SA-008 were confirmed resolved; **SA-009 came back `partially_resolved`, and the residual was consistency rather than mechanism**: revision 11's seller anchor was accepted, but **two normative sentences still described the old listing-only lock** — the MS-2a deliverable bullet, which said `SellerRatingObservation` implements the same hook as `OfferSnapshot` and `ListingScore`, and §3.4.1's coverage row for seller-rating deletion. That is the more dangerous half of an inconsistency, because MS-2a is the sub-milestone that introduces the hook: a plan reading only its own contract could have implemented exactly the listing-only instruction and reproduced the phantom, with the implementation contract and the acceptance contract pointing in opposite directions. Both sites now state the asymmetry — **`OfferSnapshot` and `ListingScore` lock listing parents only; `SellerRatingObservation` locks distinct `seller` anchors `FOR UPDATE` in ascending `id` order *first*, then the bounded listing fan-out** — and the coverage row names the anchor as the **correctness** mechanism and the listing locks as **support** for L9's cross-row guard. **MS-2a's exit criteria now carry the phantom test at historical-row level** (`pg_locks` proof that the seller lock is held; fixture 12 asserting by whole-table query that no `listing_score` row cites the deleted rating's `observed_at`, with a listing-only implementation required to go red as the negative control), and MS-2d's exit criteria name all fifteen §3.4.2 barrier fixtures and the two SA-008 first-observation cases, which no sub-milestone had previously listed. **No mechanism changes in this revision**: nothing in §3.4.2's protocol changed, and the one sentence edited there made an existing justification exact rather than altering behaviour — its "parent lock" wording became "anchor lock", named per model. No ratified decision changes meaning. **This revision carries in-house verification only**: the audit is closed, so no peer round has read it (§6, §7). **The wording pass** then closed four residual inconsistencies an in-house verifier found in revision 12 itself: §2.3(b)'s one-line summary of the serialization contract still described a single `listing` lock for every path; §6's audit row still opened "in progress" before declaring the audit closed; §7 still carried the superseded "the plan is cut when the audit converges" sentence beside the owner-decision precondition that replaced it; and this note claimed §3.4.2 was untouched when one justification sentence in it had been made exact. **The pass is the round-5 lesson applied to this revision's own text** — a late edit that updates a detailed site and leaves its summaries behind is the same failure SA-009 ended on. **The owner then accepted the in-house closure on 2026-09-06**, so the MS-2a plan precondition is met and that plan is being cut (§6, §7) — the acceptance disposes of the SA-009 wording residual only and leaves this revision's in-house-verification-only status exactly as recorded.
>
> **Finding-ID convention, because the two audits' numbering collides.** Audit `9f6079cb` numbered its findings SA-001…SA-027 across five rounds; audit `b2eedc33` restarts at SA-001. Every `SA-nnn` citation introduced in **revisions 2–7** belongs to `9f6079cb`; every one introduced in **revision 8 or later** belongs to `b2eedc33` and is written as such where the number is reused. The reused numbers are disambiguated at their older sites in §2.3(a), §3.3.2, §3.3.5(a), §3.4.1, §3.4.2 and §6, which now name `9f6079cb` explicitly. **Revision 9 adds two more collisions** — `b2eedc33`'s SA-005 (the no-eligible-snapshot availability input) and SA-006 (the refresh/sweep race) reuse numbers `9f6079cb` had already spent on the mixed-unit veto formula (§6) and on write-on-change (§3.4.2). **Revision 10 adds a third** — `b2eedc33`'s SA-007 (the seller-evidence revalidation gap) reuses the number `9f6079cb` spent on the omitted lot quantity in `$/TB` (§3.2.2, decision S2-15), disambiguated at that older site. **Revision 11 adds two more** — `b2eedc33`'s SA-008 (C5's NULL comparand) and SA-009 (the fan-out phantom) reuse numbers `9f6079cb` spent on the false `n_eff` monotonicity property (§3.2.3) and on the single scoring population (§2.2), both disambiguated at their older sites. Numbers SA-010…SA-027 remain unambiguous.

**Goal:** every listing **in the §2.2 scoring population** carries a **reproducible** 0–100 `deal_score` computed per [ADR 0011](../../adr/adr-0011-composite-deal-score.md) — the weighted geometric mean `base = Π_k max(s_k, 0.02)^{w_k}` at **price 0.50 · fitness 0.25 · seller 0.15 · availability 0.10**, gated by the three non-compensatory veto caps expressed **on the same [0,1] scale as `base`** (device-managed SMR for an enterprise/NAS buyer → `cap = 0.35`; used/seller-refurb with no returns → `cap = 0.60`; seller trust < 0.50 → `cap = 0.60`), with `deal_score = round_half_up(100 · min(base, cap))` — together with the DR-004 per-subscore explanation payload; thin cohorts (`n_eff < 30`) visibly shrink toward neutral and are marked **provisional**; and the documented cohort-relaxation fallback fires when a cohort is too small (master-spec §19 MS-2 acceptance, line 916). §3.2.1 is the normative statement of the formula and its units.

## 1. Owner decisions (proposed and ratified 2026-09-06)

Revised in revision 2 per review finding SA-001, extended in revision 3 with **S2-17, S2-18 and S2-19**, extended again in revision 4 with **S2-20, S2-21, S2-22 and S2-23**, and extended once more in revision 5 with **S2-24, S2-25 and S2-26** (review findings SA-015, SA-016, SA-018, SA-019, SA-020, SA-021 and SA-024 each turned out to need an owner-ratifiable schema or semantic choice, not only a tighter sentence). Each row was put to the owner as a **proposal** carrying an evidence-backed recommended default, the alternatives rejected, the consequence of adopting it, and an owner-disposition column.

**Ratified 2026-09-06.** The owner disposed of the whole table in one reply — *"I ratify the designs above."* — which is `accept` for **every** row (S2-1, S2-2a, S2-2b, S2-3 … S2-26; 27 rows), adopting each recommended default **verbatim**. **No row was overridden**, so no §1.2 disposition changes a normative section of this document, and — per §1.1 and the last row of §6 — **no open question was opened**: an OQ is raised only by an override whose consequence reaches spec text. Review finding SA-001 is closed by this ratification, and it is the only way it could have been closed. The dispositions below are the record; **the table's Owner disposition column is the authority** on what was decided, and the word *proposed* elsewhere in this document names the form a decision took before 2026-09-06, not its current status.

An override changes the affected sub-milestone's plan, not this decomposition, unless it moves work across a sub-milestone boundary — in which case this document is revised first (§7). None was recorded, so no such revision is required.

### 1.1 How to ratify

This is the form the ratification took, kept as the record of what the owner was asked. Reply once, one line per decision, in either form:

- `S2-N: accept` — adopt the recommended default verbatim.
- `S2-N: override — <the alternative or value you want instead>` — the named alternative, or a different constant/threshold.

A decision left unanswered stays `pending` and continues to block the MS-2a plan; an override whose consequence changes spec text rather than only MS-2 behavior is opened as an open question through the normal process (§6). **On 2026-09-06 the owner accepted the table wholesale**, so no row was left unanswered and no override was recorded — every disposition below reads `accept`, and no open question was opened by this document.

### 1.2 Ratification table

**Disposition of record: all 27 rows `accept` (owner, 2026-09-06), recommended default adopted verbatim, no override.** The Decision, Recommended default, Alternatives rejected and Consequence columns are unchanged from revision 5 — they are what was put to the owner — and revision 6 fills only the Owner disposition column.

**Reading the frozen columns against later revisions (in-house verifier, revision 9).** Because those four columns are frozen at what was ratified, a row can name a *smaller* set than the current normative text does, and S2-1 is the live example: its row lists three denormalized `listing` columns (`current_deal_score`, `current_score_provisional`, `current_scored_at`) while §2.3(b) now clears **five** — `current_cohort_key` was added in revision 5 and `current_offer_observed_at` in revision 8 (§2.1). That is a growth of the column *set*, not a change to the *decision*, which is "the current score is denormalized onto `listing` as a read-path convenience, cleared per §2.3". **§2.1 is authoritative for which columns exist; the §1.2 row is authoritative for the decision** — and the same reading applies to any other row whose enumeration a later revision extended. Where a revision changed a row's *meaning* it would need a new disposition, which is why S2-23 carries an explicit re-ratification note and no other row does.

| # | Decision (proposed) | Recommended default | Alternatives rejected | Consequence | Owner disposition |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| S2-1 | Where scores live | **A new `listing_score` TimescaleDB hypertable**, composite PK `(listing_id, scored_at)`, append-only, write-on-change (§3.4.2); plus denormalized current-score columns on `listing` (`current_deal_score`, `current_score_provisional`, `current_scored_at`) as a read-path convenience only, cleared per §2.3. | (a) **Columns on `offer_snapshot`** — the score is not an observation of the merchant, it is our conclusion about one, and a rescore triggered by a cohort shift or a grain upgrade (Appendix C.3.3: "a grain upgrade re-scores the listing") has no new observation to hang on, so it would force a synthetic snapshot row and corrupt DR-005's "re-run ⇒ new observations" invariant. (b) **One mutable current row per listing** — NFR-007 requires historical scores to be reproducible from stored inputs, and overwriting discards the very inputs (`n_eff`, `λ`, cohort membership) the reproduction needs. | New table + migration in MS-2a. Follows the existing `offer_snapshot` hypertable precedent (`catalog/migrations/0003_offer_snapshot_hypertable.py`, composite PK on `(listing_id, observed_at)`). The denormalized columns follow the `listing.product_model` / `resolution_grain` precedent in `catalog/models/market.py` — refreshed by exactly one writer, never the source of truth. **Resolves the §9 ambiguity flagged in §6** in favor of the `listing_score` table named in DR-004 and in §9's own downstream-groups paragraph. | **accept** (owner, 2026-09-06) |
| S2-2a | Retention class of scoring rows | **`listing_score` inherits its listing's `retention_class`** (it is `RetentionGoverned`), so an eBay-derived score row carries `ebay_listing_observation` and expires with the listing; every other source's score is `merchant_fact` (indefinite). **No new `RetentionClass` value is added.** | (a) A new indefinite `listing_score` class — the DR-004 payload stores "input facts used", i.e. the eBay item price and seller evidence, so an indefinite class would retain eBay observation data past the IR-002 / DR-001 ≤ 6 h freshness and delete-on-delist obligation. (b) Storing the payload without input facts to dodge the obligation — defeats DR-004 and NFR-007. | Reuses the DR-008 eBay carve-out pattern verbatim (heartbeat rows already override class TTLs per source), so `retention_constraints("listing_score")` and the existing `purge_expired` sweep cover the new table with no change to `catalog/models/base.py`. Pairs with the §2.3 clearing contract: expiry of the authoritative row must also clear the S2-1 denormalized copy. | **accept** (owner, 2026-09-06) |
| S2-2b | eBay's contribution to cohort baselines (**compliance; counsel-adjacent**) | **eBay observations are EXCLUDED from `cohort_baseline` inputs.** Baselines are built from the four non-eBay top-5 sources only; eBay listings are still **scored against** those baselines and their own `listing_score` rows stay bounded per S2-2a. `cohort_baseline` therefore holds no eBay-derived aggregate and stays `merchant_fact` / indefinite. Enforced by the §2.3 source-composition invariant and its test. | (a) **Including eBay in the aggregate and relying on the aggregate being "only a summary"** — rejected: master-spec line 404 prohibits building or acting on a derived **eBay price model**, and an indefinitely retained weighted-quantile summary of eBay prices is exactly a derived price model that survives the line-710 six-hour/delete-on-delist posture. Inferring permission from "it is only an aggregate" is the inference this design refuses to make. (b) **A bounded eBay-inclusive aggregate that expires on the same 6 h clock** — rejected here as *not this document's call*: it may well be defensible, but it is an owner/counsel decision, not a design derivation. | **Cost, stated plainly:** eBay is likely the highest-volume of the five sources, so excluding it lowers `n_eff` across the board, pushes more cohorts below 30, and makes relaxation (§3.3.2) and the provisional marker fire far more often than they would otherwise. Rankings still work — every listing is compared against the same non-eBay yardstick — but confidence is lower and MS-2d should report the size of the effect. **This is the decision most worth an owner override**, and (b) is the override to name. | **accept** (owner, 2026-09-06) |
| S2-3 | Recompute mechanism | **A dedicated scheduled `score-refresh` APScheduler job on a 60 s interval**, two-phase (refresh dirty `cohort_baseline` rows → rescore the affected listings per §3.4.1). It is **never** inline in the acquisition pipeline. | (a) **Incremental scoring inside the persist stage** — rejected twice over: the price subscore is *cohort-relative*, so one new cheap listing changes every peer's percentile and a per-listing incremental score would disagree with its own cohort's baseline within the same run; and putting a cross-source read on the per-source pipeline's critical path breaks the NFR-001 per-source isolation that `acquisition/pipeline.py` is built around. (b) **A 15-minute or hourly full recompute** — FR-001's tightest freshness SLO is p95 ≤ 3 min transition-to-alert for drop-prone sources with a cheap signal, and MS-4 watch matching consumes the score, so a 15-minute scoring lag would consume the entire budget before the alert path starts. | One `scheduler.add_job(..., "interval", seconds=SCORE_REFRESH_SECONDS, id="score-refresh")` alongside the existing `retention-sweep` / `bucket-checkpoint` jobs in `poller/service.py:build_scheduler`. The existing `job_defaults={"max_instances": 1, "coalesce": True}` already gives the idempotence and overlap protection this needs. §14 runtime bound: the full refresh must complete inside its own 60 s interval at v1 scale; MS-2d measures and records it (see S2-9). | **accept** (owner, 2026-09-06) |
| S2-4 | Cohort dirtiness | **Derived, not queued**: a cohort's dirtiness is computed from stored dependency stamps at each tick, per the complete model in §3.4.1 — which covers new observations, window-boundary exits, retention deletions, refdata changes, membership changes, and version bumps. Revision 3 removed the decay staleness ceiling that revision 2 listed here: exponential decay is scale-invariant, so time alone changes nothing (SA-014, §3.4.1). | A `score_recompute_queue` table written by the persist stage — a second source of truth for something already derivable, it re-couples scoring to the acquisition path (contradicting S2-3), and a lost enqueue silently freezes a cohort's scores with no detectable symptom. | No new coupling and no queue table; the dirty check is a bounded set of indexed aggregate queries per refresh. A cohort with no dependency change and a fresh-enough baseline is skipped entirely, so a quiet system does near-zero work at 60 s. **Round-1 finding SA-003 was against the round-1 wording** ("dirty iff a newer snapshot exists"), which was incomplete; §3.4.1 is the replacement. | **accept** (owner, 2026-09-06) |
| S2-5 | Where cohort statistics are computed | **In Python**, inside a pure `scoring/` library, fed by one bounded SQL pull per refresh (the 90-day window of `(listing_id, observed_at, USD landed price, capacity_tb, quantity, cohort key parts, source_site)`). | (a) A **TimescaleDB continuous aggregate** — rejected on a hard technical ground, not preference: ADR 0011's weights are `w = 2^(−age_days/30)`, a **now-relative** exponential decay, and a continuous aggregate materializes over fixed time buckets. Every weight changes on every tick, so the materialization would be stale by construction and would have to be fully re-materialized each run anyway. The cohort key also requires joins through `product_variant` → `product_model` → `drive_spec`, which is fragile inside a CAGG definition. (b) **SQL window functions** — `percentile_cont` computes an *unweighted* percentile; there is no built-in weighted-percentile aggregate, so the decay would have to be emulated by row duplication. | Mirrors the Appendix C.3 "pure-function library plus a DB-facing service" split that `matching/` already implements (`matching/ladder.py` pure, `matching/resolver.py` DB-facing). The whole ADR-0011 math becomes table-testable with no database — including the mock-data archetypes the ADR was ratified on. Reopen if profiling at MS-2d shows the SQL pull, not the Python, dominates the refresh budget. | **accept** (owner, 2026-09-06) |
| S2-6 | Where score-affecting constants live | **Versioned code under `algorithm_version`** (§3.4.3), not in ADR-0016 settings rows. | The ADR-0016 settings-row pattern used for cadence and matcher thresholds — rejected here specifically: NFR-007 requires a historical score to be reproducible from stored inputs, and a mutable row changed by `UPDATE` silently invalidates every score computed before the change unless the change also bumps a version — at which point the version, not the row, is doing the work. | `algorithm_version` is a stored column on every `listing_score` row (DR-004: "algorithm version; thresholds and margins"). A constant change is a code change, a version bump, and a full rescore. The tunables ADR 0016 legitimately owns (poll cadence, occurrence thresholds) are untouched. The constant set this covers is enumerated in §3.4.3. | **accept** (owner, 2026-09-06) |
| S2-7 | A listing with no usable capacity | **Scores `s_price = 0.5` with `price_basis = "capacity_unavailable"`, `λ = 0`, and the `provisional` marker set** — it is still scored. | (a) **Not scoring the listing** — §19 MS-2 acceptance (line 916) says **every** listing has a 0–100 score; §2.2 bounds "every" to the scoring population, and a listing in that population is always scored. (b) **`s_price = 0`** — a missing input is not evidence of a bad deal, and the 0.50 price weight would drive the listing below genuinely overpriced peers and poison the MS-3 dashboard ordering. | 0.5 is not an invention: it is exactly the limit of ADR 0011's own formula `s_price = λ·(1−q) + (1−λ)·0.5` as `λ → 0`, which is the mechanism the ADR already prescribes for a cohort with no usable evidence. The explanation payload states the reason, so the glass box stays honest (FR-006). §3.2.6 enumerates all four `price_basis` values and the `provisional_reasons` each one emits; the `provisional` marker is set here because a listing with no cohort records `n_eff = 0`, so it satisfies §3.2.6's single Boolean rather than needing a second rule. | **accept** (owner, 2026-09-06) |
| S2-8 | Cap posture on unknown values | **Caps fire only on affirmative evidence; `unknown` never triggers a cap** — it raises a risk flag instead. Concretely: `recording_tech = smr_dm` caps at 0.35; `smr_hm` and `smr_unknown` do not (flag only); `returns_policy = no_returns` on a used/seller-refurb variant caps at 0.60; `unknown` does not (flag only). | Treating `unknown` as the unsafe value ("fail closed") — a cap is a hard, non-compensatory disqualifier, so a false positive is far more damaging than a false negative, and this repository already ratified that posture for identity in ADR 0019 (only deterministic rungs auto-accept; C.3.5 "a false merge corrupts … a miss only queues"). Host-managed SMR is additionally a *deliberate* enterprise choice, not the trap the ADR names. | The risk-flag list is a first-class part of the DR-004 payload, so an unknown-SMR or unknown-returns listing is visibly qualified in the MS-3 listing detail rather than silently capped. **Verified reach limit:** `smr_dm` is only ever available at model grain (§3.3.3), so the SMR veto is structurally model-grain-only — below model grain every SMR listing lands on the flag-only path by construction, not by choice. If the owner prefers fail-closed, it is a one-constant change under S2-6 plus a version bump. | **accept** (owner, 2026-09-06) |
| S2-9 | Delivery shape | **Approach A decomposition + PR per sub-milestone** — five plans, five `dev→main` PRs, mirroring MS-1's S-6. | A single MS-2 plan — the pure math library and the DB/scheduler service have disjoint verification strategies (table-driven no-DB tests vs. live-TimescaleDB tests), and MS-1's five-PR cadence is the established repository rhythm. | Five plan documents under `docs/superpowers/plans/`, five PRs; `main` (and prod) advances per increment. | **accept** (owner, 2026-09-06) |
| S2-10 | Dependency on ADR-0019 ratification | **MS-2 does not wait for the MS-1e / ADR-0019 ratification.** | Blocking MS-2 on ADR 0019 flipping `proposed → accepted` — §19's MS-2 acceptance (line 916) names the ≥ 80% model-grain figure as "a coverage **expectation, not a gate** — C.3.5; scoring cohorts consume the resolved grain", and names no ratification dependency; the ratification is MS-1's own acceptance criterion (line 908). | MS-2 proceeds against whatever resolution state exists. Below-model-grain behavior is specified in §3.3.3: listings under model grain fall back to persisted N2-extracted attributes at a lower provenance, or to S2-7's neutral price subscore; the shortfall is *reported* by the MS-2e coverage report and routed to the catalog/matcher backlog — C.3.5: "shortfall signals catalog/rule gaps, never a reason to loosen precision." | **accept** (owner, 2026-09-06) |
| S2-11 | Missing shipping | **A flag, not a numeric haircut, at MS-2.** | Applying a percentage penalty to `$/TB` when `shipping_price IS NULL` — C.4 says "missing shipping → penalty/flag" but neither ADR 0011 nor the spec fixes a magnitude, and inventing one would silently change every affected listing's rank. | `shipping_unknown` enters the risk flags and the user-facing explanation text; `$/TB` is computed from `total_landed_price` as it stands (item + known shipping + known tax). If the owner wants a haircut, it is a constant under S2-6. | **accept** (owner, 2026-09-06) |
| S2-12 | Per-source seller policy priors | **`SourceConfig` rows** (ADR 0016 row pattern), defaulting to **0.60 for eBay** (major marketplace) and **0.50 for every other source**, per C.4. | Hard-coding the priors — unlike S2-6's score-shaping constants, the prior is a *per-source classification* judgement the owner should be able to change per source without a deploy, and the row value is stamped into the explanation payload and the §3.4.1 input digest so reproducibility survives. | New `SourceConfig.seller_policy_prior` float column in MS-2a. **Load-bearing arithmetic detail:** the veto is `seller trust < 0.50` (strict), so the 0.50 "other" prior sits exactly on the boundary and does **not** trip the cap. An implementation using `<=` would cap every first-party merchant listing at 60. MS-2b carries an explicit boundary test at 0.50. | **accept** (owner, 2026-09-06) |
| S2-13 | Reproducing historical scores after a version bump | **An immutable version-dispatch contract**: every persisted `algorithm_version` keeps an executable, frozen module under `scoring/versions/`, with per-version golden fixtures run on every change (§3.4.3). | A **per-row reproducibility manifest** embedding the full constant set in `inputs_json` — rejected: constants are only half the algorithm. The *code path* — rubric structure, relaxation order, rounding mode, digest canonicalization — cannot be embedded in a row, so a manifest would guarantee the constants and still not guarantee reproduction, which is precisely what NFR-007 asks for. | One package per released version plus a two-digest manifest (directory + registry) and a CI immutability check; the version directory grows monotonically until the §3.4.3 item-5 **operational** retirement procedure proves that no authoritative dataset — production **or** any retained restorable backup (§18.6: 48 hourly / 14 daily / 8 weekly / 6 monthly) — cites the version. CI cannot perform that proof and does not try. Costs a little duplication; buys NFR-007 literally. **New decision in revision 2** (review finding SA-010). | **accept** (owner, 2026-09-06) |
| S2-14 | Terminal behavior when relaxation is exhausted | **The terminal cohort stays provisional with its actual `n_eff`** and `λ = min(1, n_eff/30)`; it does **not** collapse to the neutral price subscore. The neutral path (S2-7) is reserved for a cohort with **zero** usable observations. | **Falling back to neutral price when the tier-relaxed cohort still has `n_eff < 30`** — rejected: ADR 0011's warm-up shrinkage is already the designed answer to thin evidence, and `s_price = λ·(1−q) + (1−λ)·0.5` degrades continuously toward 0.5 as `n_eff → 0`. A hard switch would discard real evidence and put a discontinuity in the score surface at an arbitrary threshold. | `relaxation_step = "tier"` plus `relaxation_exhausted = true` on the baseline and in the payload; the listing is visibly provisional with its true `n_eff`. Under S2-2b this path will be common early. **New decision in revision 2** (review finding SA-004). | **accept** (owner, 2026-09-06) |
| S2-15 | Lot quantity in `$/TB` | **`$/TB` divides by validated lot quantity** (master-spec line 1200): `dollars_per_tb = usd_total_landed_price / (capacity_tb_per_unit × quantity)`. Quantity comes from the existing N2 extractor, persisted per §3.3.3, with the missing/invalid/low-confidence semantics in §3.2.2. Cohort membership still uses **per-unit** capacity. | **Ignoring quantity** (the round-1 wording) — contradicts master-spec line 1200 outright and scores a 4-pack as four times more expensive than it is, corrupting the cohort distribution it also feeds. | No new extractor: `matching/vocab.py` `extract()` already returns `quantity` as an `Attribute[int]` with confidence, layer and `source_text`. MS-2a/2c must **persist** that output (§3.3.3) — today it is computed in `matching/resolver.py` and discarded. **New decision in revision 2** (review finding SA-007). | **accept** (owner, 2026-09-06) |
| S2-16 | Which listings are in scope | **One population predicate** (§2.2): a top-5-source listing that is `active()` (not delisted, not past `expires_at`) and has at least one `offer_snapshot`. A listing leaving the population has its denormalized score fields cleared (§2.3); it is rescored on re-entry. | (a) **"Every listing ever persisted"** — would require scoring redacted, delisted and expired rows whose merchant content is gone by contract. (b) **"Every listing with a snapshot, including delisted"** — retains and displays a current score for an offer that no longer exists, and for eBay conflicts with delete-on-delist. | The goal statement, the MS-2d exit criterion, and the MS-2 final acceptance all quantify over this one predicate. Note the deliberate asymmetry with baselines: the *observation* population feeding a baseline is broader in time (90 days of non-eBay snapshots, including those of listings that have since left the population) — §2.2 states why. **New decision in revision 2** (review finding SA-009). | **accept** (owner, 2026-09-06) |
| S2-17 | The v1 fitness and availability rubric constants | **The exhaustive numeric tables in §3.2.4** — `T` as `TIER_BASE × TECH_FACTOR` for HDD and `TIER_BASE × DWPD_FACTOR` for SSD (with the stated DWPD class boundaries), the 4 × 6 `W` table applied to *claimed* warranty with a `warranty_unverified` flag on every MS-2 row, the 7 × 4 `C` table, and availability at `preorder = 0.30` / `unknown = 0.50` alongside ADR 0011's fixed 1.00 / 0.00 endpoints. Seeded from `docs/research/principled-deal-score-for-hard-drive-listings.md:249-279`, with every seed→current-vocabulary mapping stated in §3.2.4. | (a) **Leaving them as "versioned constants" without values** (revision 2) — review finding SA-011: naming where a constant lives does not select it, and materially different rankings all satisfied the document, so MS-2b could not implement the 35% fitness/availability portion reproducibly. (b) **Copying the seed rubric verbatim** — its categories (`NAS Pro`, `desktop CMR`, `backorder_7d`, `seller refurb w/SMART proof`) have no counterpart in the current `MarketTier` / `StockStatus` / `RecertChannel` vocabularies, so a verbatim copy is not implementable either. (c) **Deriving the values from real observations** — that is re-fitting, which §3.2 puts explicitly out of MS-2 scope. | These are the numbers that decide the fitness ranking, so this is the decision an owner is most likely to want to move cell by cell. An override is a constant change, a version bump, and a full rescore — no schema or plan-boundary impact. The tables enter the §3.4.3 constant set and the per-version golden fixtures, and MS-2b's exhaustive fixtures assert their counts against the enums themselves, so a later enum addition fails until its cell exists. **New decision in revision 3** (review finding SA-011). | **accept** (owner, 2026-09-06) |
| S2-18 | What a cohort baseline persists | **The §3.3.4 `observation_vector`** — the ordered `[ln_price_per_tb, w_rel, listing_id, event_at]` array, one element per **price event** (the first eligible snapshot of each equal-price run in the listing's full retained history, left-censored into the window per S2-25), with `w_rel` **anchored to the newest price event** rather than to `now` or to the newest raw snapshot. It is a sufficient statistic: any listing's exact `q` is recomputable from the stored row alone. | (a) **Revision 2's "quantile points" summary** — review finding SA-012: an arbitrary listing's exact weighted rank needs the ordered value/weight distribution, so a listing-only rescore under L2/L4/L5 could not reproduce its own prior `q` without re-running the 90-day pull. (b) **Mandating an observation re-pull on every listing-only rescore** — turns a single-listing seller-rating update into a full cohort query, and makes the L4/L5 fan-out (every listing for a seller or a source) quadratic. (c) **Storing `now`-relative weights** — they change every tick, so the baseline digest would change every tick and SA-014's write amplification would come straight back through a different door. (d) **One element per snapshot** — an unchanged listing would contribute thousands of identical prices over 90 days and dominate the weighted distribution by sitting still; that is a defect in the statistic before it is a storage cost. | A JSON array column on `cohort_baseline` sized at roughly 10³ elements per cohort at v1 (§3.3.4 states the bound and its derivation), guarded by `BASELINE_VECTOR_MAX_ELEMENTS` (proposed **20 000**) that fails a cohort into the §4 isolation path rather than truncating a distribution. MS-2c measures the real size distribution before MS-2d runs it in production. Because the vector names its contributing `listing_id`s, the S2-2b eBay exclusion must stay enforced at the SQL-pull level. **New decision in revision 3** (review finding SA-012). | **accept** (owner, 2026-09-06) |
| S2-19 | How a global score refresh is recorded | **A new `ScoringRun` model** in `catalog/models/ops.py` with **no source field at all** (§3.4.4): started/finished, `RunStatus`, `RunFailureClass`, `algorithm_version`, and the six counts (`cohorts_evaluated`, `cohorts_refreshed`, `baseline_writes`, `listings_rescored`, `score_writes`, `baseline_versions_pruned`). `RunKind.SCORE` is **not** added. | (a) **Revision 2's `RunKind.SCORE` on `ScraperRun`** — review finding SA-013: `ScraperRun.source_site` is a non-null `PROTECT` FK (`catalog/models/ops.py:206-225`), so the refresh is cross-source and the row it was told to write cannot exist. (b) **Making `source_site` nullable** — weakens an invariant that currently holds for every acquisition run and silently changes the meaning of the existing `("source_site", "-started_at")` index and of every source-health query, putting the cost on MS-1 code unrelated to scoring. (c) **A sentinel non-merchant `SourceSite` row** — a fake merchant in a table carrying retention class, licensing posture and poll cadence; it would surface in operator source lists as a source that cannot be polled. | One migration and one model. `ScraperRun` is untouched, so no source-health, cadence or lane query needs a `run_kind != score` filter — the non-interference is structural rather than something every query has to remember. The counts also make the SA-014 quiet-system claim auditable in production: a quiet tick shows `cohorts_evaluated > 0` with every write count at zero. Revision 4 adds `baseline_versions_pruned` for the §3.3.5(g) sweep. **New decision in revision 3** (review finding SA-013). | **accept** (owner, 2026-09-06) |
| S2-20 | How a cohort baseline is versioned | **Append-only baseline versions plus a separate current-pointer table** (§3.3.5): one immutable `cohort_baseline` row per `(cohort_key, baseline_digest)` with its own `computed_at`; the mutable "which version is current" pointer lives in a small `cohort_baseline_current` table keyed by `cohort_key`. Every `listing_score` carries a `PROTECT` FK to the exact immutable version it was scored against, **and** §3.4.1 group 4 carries the sufficient price evidence so the score re-derives without the baseline row at all. | (a) **Revision 3's one mutable upserted row per key** — review finding SA-015: a later semantic refresh overwrites the `observation_vector` an older score was computed from, so the older row's FK names a baseline that is no longer the one whose digest it stored, and the promised NFR-007 re-derivation cannot compute the historical price subscore. (b) **A `is_current` boolean on the version row with a partial unique index** (`UNIQUE(cohort_key) WHERE is_current`) — rejected: promoting a new version requires **updating** the previous immutable row to clear its flag, so the row is no longer write-once, the "immutable version" invariant becomes "immutable except for one column", and every immutability test has to carve out an exception that a later refactor can widen. A separate pointer table keeps the version rows strictly write-once and the DB constraint (`cohort_key` primary key on the pointer) just as strong. (c) **Embedding the whole `observation_vector` in every score's `inputs_json`** — ~10³ elements per score row instead of per cohort; the score row is the highest-cardinality object in the system. | New table + FK in MS-2a; §3.3.5 states the retention class (`merchant_fact`, indefinite — lawful only because S2-2b keeps eBay out of the vector) and the prune rule (`PROTECT` makes the invariant structural; the sweep only reclaims versions nothing cites). **Cost, stated plainly:** an active cohort produces a new version on every price event and every window exit, so version rows accumulate where revision 3 rewrote one row. §3.3.5 gives the derived growth estimate and **makes an MS-2c measurement a precondition for enabling MS-2d in production**. **New decision in revision 4** (review finding SA-015). | **accept** (owner, 2026-09-06) |
| S2-21 | The seller-feedback provider contract | **The §3.2.5 contract**: `p_obs = Decimal(feedbackPercentage)/100` quantized to 4 dp and clamped to `[0,1]`; `n = int(feedbackScore)` clamped at `≥ 0` and used as a **total-count proxy**; `y = p_obs · n` quantized to 4 dp; `n = 0` or either field missing/malformed ⇒ the S2-12 no-rating state, never a fabricated zero-positive seller. `SellerRatingObservation` stores the raw provider values `rating_percent_raw` / `feedback_count_raw` alongside the normalized `p_obs` / `n` / `y` / `count_basis` / `is_usable`, and the seller aggregate is the **single latest `observed_at` row regardless of `is_usable`** (revision 5, SA-016), never a sum of deltas. | (a) Revision 3's `positive_count` / `total_count` / `rating_percent` triple — review finding SA-016: eBay publishes a percentage and a count, not a positive count, so two of the three columns could only be back-computed, and a back-computed `positive_count` re-derived from a rounded percentage is not an observation. (b) **Summing or differencing repeated observations** — the provider republishes a cumulative state, so differencing it treats a republication as new evidence and double-counts. (c) **Averaging across observations** — mixes provider windows and makes the score depend on poll cadence. | Rewrites one MS-2a model and one MS-2e adapter change; no scoring-math change. **The one thing this decision cannot settle from repository evidence** is whether eBay's `feedbackScore` is a *total* feedback count or a *net* (positives minus negatives) score — §3.2.5 states the contract conditional on that confirmation and makes the confirmation an MS-2e plan-time gate, exactly as §3, MS-2e already does for return terms. **New decision in revision 4** (review finding SA-016). | **accept** (owner, 2026-09-06) |
| S2-22 | Which snapshot supplies a listing's current offer facts | **The §2.2.1 selector**: among the listing's `offer_snapshot` rows, the **eligible** ones (non-null `usd_total_landed_price`, i.e. a non-null `fx_rate`) with the **greatest `observed_at`**. Ties are impossible — `observed_at` is half of the composite primary key. Selection is by `observed_at`, never by insertion order, so a late-arriving *older* snapshot never displaces a newer one; it still enters the 90-day baseline window. Deletion or expiry of the selected row triggers reselection and a rescore (trigger **L8**). | (a) **Insertion order / greatest primary-key sequence** — `offer_snapshot` has no surrogate sequence (composite PK on `(listing_id, observed_at)`, verified at `catalog/models/market.py:510-555`), and even if it had one, a backfilled older observation would silently become "current". (b) **The snapshot that last extended `expires_at`** — couples the current price to the retention clock, so a heartbeat-only re-observation would repoint the price. (c) **Leaving it implicit** — review finding SA-018: revision 3's `P` required only that *at least one* snapshot exist, so two conforming implementations could score the same stored listing differently. | No schema change: the selector is a query, pinned in §2.2.1 and used by §3.4.1 group 1 and by the §2.2 population predicate. Adds one rescore trigger (L8) and its isolation test. **New decision in revision 4** (review finding SA-018). | **accept** (owner, 2026-09-06); revision 9 states in §2.2.1 and §3.4.1 group 1 what the **absence** of a selection means for the non-price offer facts, per SA-005 — the selector `S(listing)`, its eligibility predicate and its tie/ordering rules are **unchanged**, and no fact is read from an unselected row |
| S2-23 | What `is_provisional` means | **`is_provisional := (n_eff < 30)`, and nothing else** (§3.2.6), with an ordered `provisional_reasons` list (`thin_cohort`, `capacity_unavailable`, `zero_observations`, `price_unavailable`) that **refines but never widens** the Boolean. `warranty_unverified` is a **risk flag only** and never contributes to `is_provisional`. | **Revision 3's implicit OR with `warranty_unverified`** — review finding SA-019: every MS-2 row carries `warranty_unverified` because the `verification_event` lookup is unscheduled (C.4), so ORing it in makes **every** score provisional regardless of cohort strength, which contradicts §19's acceptance wording, the MS-2d exit criterion, and the MS-2e reporting denominator that all read provisional as the thin-cohort signal. Two orthogonal uncertainties needed two names, not one Boolean. | One Boolean expression, one enumerated reason list, and a four-combination test (`n_eff` above/below 30 × warranty verified/unverified). The reasons are all subsets of `n_eff < 30`, so the Boolean stays single-sourced and the explanation still says *why*. **New decision in revision 4** (review finding SA-019). | **accept** (owner, 2026-09-06); enumeration completed in revision 7 per SA-019 — Boolean semantics unchanged; amendment re-ratified by the owner 2026-09-06 |
| S2-24 | How an inserted observation is detected | **A server-assigned insertion stamp: `OfferSnapshot.ingested_at`** (§2.2.2), `db_default=Now()`, never accepted from a caller, indexed. B1 becomes a high-water mark on `ingested_at` (with the §2.2.2 commit-lag margin) instead of on `observed_at`; the existing rows are backfilled `ingested_at := observed_at` and marked `ingest_stamp_backfilled = true`. | (a) **Keeping `observed_at` as the insertion cursor** (revision 4) — review finding SA-020: `acquisition/persist.py:74-102` takes `observed_at` from the caller and appends directly, and `catalog/models/market.py:510-555` carries no other insertion evidence, so a snapshot backfilled with an older merchant timestamp is invisible to a `observed_at > last_evaluated_at` test and its cohort stays stale indefinitely. (b) **A `bigserial` ingestion sequence** — equivalent in ordering but a second surrogate key on a hypertable whose PK is deliberately composite, and it has exactly the same pre-commit assignment race as a timestamp, so it buys nothing the lag margin does not already handle. (c) **Trusting B3's digest alone** — B3 does catch it, but B3 is the full-scan backstop; without B1 every cohort would need the expensive path on every tick. | One column, one index and one data migration in MS-2a; `persist.append_snapshot` is **not** changed (it must not pass the field). Backfilled rows are honestly marked: their `ingested_at` is a reconstruction, so an audit can tell a real stamp from an inferred one. **New decision in revision 5** (review finding SA-020). | **accept** (owner, 2026-09-06) |
| S2-25 | What a price event is, relative to the window | **A run boundary in the listing's full retained history, carried into the window left-censored** (§3.3.4): an event is the first eligible snapshot of an equal-price run over the listing's whole retained history, the run contributes to the window if any of its snapshots falls inside it, and a run that started before the window keeps its **true start time** as the element's `event_at`. The weight anchor is the newest **event**, not the newest snapshot. | (a) **Revision 4's window-local definition** — review finding SA-021: with `E` computed inside the rolling window, each raw snapshot of a long unchanged run exits in poll order and the next identical snapshot becomes the representative, so `observed_at` and `w_rel` move and a new baseline version plus a cohort-wide rescore is produced **every poll interval**. (b) **Clamping the carry-in element to the window start** and weighting it there — rejected on the SA-014 invariance: the window start is `now`-relative, so the clamped element's weight would move on every tick while every other element's stayed fixed. That is not a common factor, so it changes `q`, `n_eff` and the digest continuously — the same write amplification through a new door. (c) **Anchoring the whole window to the newest observation** (`anchor − 90 d`) — it removes `now` entirely and is tempting, but a cohort that stops receiving observations then freezes its evidence forever instead of ageing out, and B2 would have to be withdrawn. | No schema change beyond the two counts already on the version; it changes which rows `E` yields and therefore the §3.3.5(h) growth estimate and the §4 capacity note, both recomputed under this definition. **Cost, stated plainly:** a price that has not moved in more than 90 days is weighted at its true (old) age, so it stays in the distribution but contributes little; the alternative readings either churn (b) or never age (c). **New decision in revision 5** (review finding SA-021). | **accept** (owner, 2026-09-06) |
| S2-26 | The baseline FK on a neutral-price score | **Nullable exactly for the enumerated no-baseline `price_basis` values, under a database CHECK** (§3.3.5c): `cohort_baseline_id IS NULL ⇔ price_basis ∈ {capacity_unavailable, price_unavailable, no_cohort_evidence}`, and `cohort_key IS NULL ⇔ price_basis = capacity_unavailable`. | (a) **A mandatory FK with a synthetic empty baseline version per cohort key** — it keeps one constraint shape, but it manufactures an immutable evidence row that summarizes no evidence, gives it a digest and a retention class, and puts it on the prune path; a reader cannot then distinguish "scored against an empty cohort" from "scored against a cohort that happens to be empty right now" without reading the vector anyway. (b) **A nullable FK with no CHECK** (the revision-4 silence) — review finding SA-024: nothing then prevents a `cohort`-basis score with no baseline, which is the exact row that breaks the NFR-007 audit path. | One nullable FK, two CHECK constraints, and one MS-2a test per neutral path. A NULL FK cites no version, so these rows never protect a baseline version from the §3.3.5(f) prune — stated there. Retention is unchanged (S2-2a). **New decision in revision 5** (review finding SA-024). | **accept** (owner, 2026-09-06) |

**Scope fence (unchanged from the brief and §19):** MS-2 scores the top-5 recert sources' listings only. No new sources, no alerting (MS-4), no UI (MS-3) — MS-2 produces the payload FR-006 says MS-3 renders.

## 2. Module layout

New code under `src/hw_radar/`, split on the same seam `matching/` already uses — a pure library plus one DB-facing service:

```
hw_radar/
├── scoring/                     # MS-2b + MS-2c + MS-2d
│   ├── contracts.py             # Pydantic v2: CohortKey, CohortStats, SubscoreSet,
│   │                            #   ScoreExplanation, ScoredListing (the DR-004 payload
│   │                            #   shape) — mirrors acquisition/contracts.py's style.
│   │                            #   CURRENT-VERSION FAÇADE ONLY: no released version
│   │                            #   package imports it (§3.4.3 item 1)
│   ├── cohort.py                # PURE: cohort-key derivation, capacity bucketing,
│   │                            #   and the relaxation ladder (§3.3.1, §3.3.2)
│   ├── stats.py                 # PURE: w = 2^(-age_days/30); n_eff = (Σw)²/Σ(w²);
│   │                            #   ascending weighted price rank q on ln($/TB),
│   │                            #   half-weight ties; λ = min(1, n_eff/30)
│   ├── subscores.py             # PURE: price / fitness / seller / availability rubrics,
│   │                            #   including the §3.2.2 quantity-aware $/TB
│   ├── vetoes.py                # PURE: the three non-compensatory caps + risk flags
│   ├── dispatch.py             # PURE: ALGORITHM_VERSIONS[v].score(D) and NOTHING
│   │                            #   else — the whole historical execution path
│   │                            #   (§3.4.3 item 2, SA-010)
│   ├── compose.py               # PURE: CURRENT-VERSION FAÇADE ONLY — builds D for a
│   │                            #   new score; never on a re-derivation path
│   ├── digest.py                # PURE: canonical JSON + SHA-256 for the input
│   │                            #   fingerprint (§3.4.1) and decision digest (§3.4.2)
│   ├── explain.py               # PURE: machine payload + user-facing text (FR-006)
│   ├── versions/                # PURE, IMMUTABLE once released (S2-13)
│   │   ├── __init__.py          #   STATIC algorithm_version → package registry; its own
│   │   │                        #   digest is recorded in EVERY manifest entry (§3.4.3)
│   │   ├── v1/                  #   the complete v1 constant set, its version-local
│   │   │                        #   parsing, helpers, and the score(D: dict) -> dict
│   │   │                        #   entry point — a frozen dependency closure with no
│   │   │                        #   shared-module imports at all (§3.4.3)
│   │   └── MANIFEST.json        #   per-version DIRECTORY digest + registry digest,
│   │                            #   gate-checked against the merge base (§3.4.3)
│   ├── baseline.py              # DB-facing: dirty-cohort detection, the 90-day SQL pull,
│   │                            #   cohort_baseline writes, source-composition invariant
│   └── service.py               # DB-facing: ScoringService — rescore a cohort, write
│                                #   listing_score, refresh listing denorm columns
├── poller/service.py            # + the score-refresh job (S2-3)
├── matching/resolver.py         # + persist N2 extraction on the listing (§3.3.3)
├── acquisition/sources/ebay.py  # + seller feedback and return terms (§3, MS-2e)
└── catalog/models/
    ├── scoring.py               # NEW: ListingScore, CohortBaseline (append-only
    │                            #   versions), CohortBaselineCurrent (the pointer),
    │                            #   SellerRatingObservation
    ├── market.py                # + Listing.returns_policy / returns_window_days /
    │                            #   extracted_attrs_json / current_deal_score /
    │                            #   current_score_provisional / current_scored_at /
    │                            #   current_cohort_key / scoring_inputs_changed_at;
    │                            #   OfferSnapshot.usd_total_landed_price /
    │                            #   ingested_at (§2.2.2) / ingest_stamp_backfilled
    ├── identity.py              # DriveSpec.market_tier → TextChoices (MarketTier)
    └── ops.py                   # + ScoringRun (S2-19); SourceConfig.seller_policy_prior
```

Everything except `baseline.py`, `service.py` and the models is a **pure-function library** — no I/O, no Django imports beyond types — so the entire ADR-0011 math is table-testable against the mock-data archetypes with no database, exactly as `matching/ladder.py` is testable without one.

### 2.1 MS-2 data-model additions (fields ADR 0011 needs that do not exist today)

Verified against `src/hw_radar/catalog/models/` and `src/hw_radar/matching/` at `17310c3`. Each row is a migration or a field addition in MS-2a.

| Need (ADR 0011 / C.4) | Today | MS-2 addition |

Revision 3's `positive_count` / `total_count` / `rating_percent` triple is **withdrawn** — no top-5 provider publishes a positive *count* (review finding SA-016) |
