Condition 2 is enforced by the **database**, not by the query: the `PROTECT` FK makes the delete fail rather than silently orphan a score, so a prune query with a bug degrades to "deletes nothing" instead of "makes history underivable". The sweep treats a `ProtectedError` as the expected outcome for a still-cited version, counts it, and moves on.

Because bounded eBay `listing_score` rows expire under S2-2a while `merchant_fact` scores do not, in steady state the versions actually reclaimed are (i) those superseded before any score cited them — two semantic changes inside one tick, or a cohort with no members in `P` — and (ii) those whose only citations were eBay rows that have since expired. **A version cited by a `merchant_fact` score is retained for as long as that score is**, which is the correct outcome: it is the evidence the score is audited against.

**(g) The sweep.** Pruning runs **inside the `score-refresh` job** (S2-3), at most once per `BASELINE_PRUNE_INTERVAL` (a constant under S2-6, proposed **1 hour**), after the refresh's own phases, and reports `baseline_versions_pruned` on the `ScoringRun` row (§3.4.4). *Rejected alternative:* folding it into the existing hourly `retention-sweep`. Rejected because that sweep's tested invariant is "deletes only bounded `RetentionGoverned` rows"; deleting `merchant_fact` storage-hygiene rows there would weaken an invariant MS-1 already tests, and it would split ownership of the baseline lifecycle across two jobs. Keeping it in the scoring job leaves exactly one writer for `cohort_baseline`.

**(h) The growth this costs, recomputed under the §3.3.4 event definition (S2-25).** Append-only turns "rewrite one row per cohort" into "insert a row per semantic change per cohort", and §3.3.4 sizes a vector at ~10³ elements, tens of kilobytes. The version-creation rate is therefore the **rate of semantic change**, and revision 5 is the first revision in which that rate is actually bounded by price movement:

| Quantity | Derivation | Order |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Members per cohort / cohorts at v1 | This document's standing v1 assumption | ~10² / ~10² |
| Genuine price changes per member per day | Recert prices move on a scale of days to weeks (§3.3.4) ⇒ ~0.1/day | 10⁻¹ |
| Window exits per cohort per day | Bounded by listings ceasing to be polled; same order or below | ≤ 10¹ |
| Source-attribution changes per cohort per day | Renames and re-attributions are administrative events, not polling events (§2.3a, new identity term in revision 7) | ≲ 10⁻² |
| **New versions per cohort per day** | members × changes + exits + source-attribution changes | **~10¹** (the added term is three orders below the price-change term) |
| **Bytes per day across the table** | 10¹ versions × 10² cohorts × ~30 KB | **~10¹ MB/day** |

**Revision 7 widens the version identity and does not move this estimate.** Folding the semantic `source_composition` into `baseline_digest` (SA-025) adds a minting term that fires on a source rename or a re-attribution — administrative events orders below the price-change rate — while the same revision removes the two poll-cadence quantities revision 6 kept in the version's own columns, `raw_snapshot_count` and `next_window_exit_at`, to the pointer. The estimate would have moved had the whole revision-6 composition map been folded in; §3.3.5(b) rejects exactly that, and for exactly this reason. The numbers match revision 4's, and that is the point worth stating plainly: revision 4 **derived** its estimate from price events while its own definition of `E` created a version per **poll**. At a 60 s–15 min cadence that is 10²–10³ versions per cohort per day — order **10³ MB/day**, two orders above the estimate §4 was carrying, on a system where nothing changed. SA-021 is exactly that gap between the estimate and the mechanism; S2-25 closes it by making the mechanism produce what the estimate assumed. The three bounds still apply in order: the digest is content-addressed, so a no-op refresh writes nothing at all (the SA-014 quiet-system property doing the work here too); the prune rule reclaims uncited versions; and `BASELINE_VECTOR_MAX_ELEMENTS` fails an oversized cohort into the §4 isolation path rather than storing it.

**This is not left to production to discover.** MS-2c already measures the vector-size distribution (S2-18); revision 4 extended that obligation, and revision 5 keeps it unchanged because a corrected derivation is still a derivation: **MS-2c must also report measured version-creation rate and measured bytes per day per cohort, and MS-2d must not be enabled in production until that measurement is recorded against the §4 capacity note and the CT-116 disk alert.** If the measurement exceeds the budget, the named follow-up is to release the `observation_vector` (set it NULL, stamping `vector_compacted_at`) on non-current versions past a retention horizon — the score stays re-derivable because (d) puts the sufficient evidence on the score row, and only the independent-audit path is lost. That change is **not adopted here**: it trades away an audit capability, which is an owner call, and it should be made against a measurement rather than against this estimate. Since revision 8 it is also a **schema** change and not only a policy one — releasing the vector is an `UPDATE`, and §3.3.5(a)'s immutability trigger refuses every `UPDATE` on this table, so adopting the follow-up means replacing that trigger with a narrowed one in the same change, never disabling it (review finding SA-003).

**(i) When a cohort loses its last usable observation — the `baseline-void` state and its two transitions.**

Review finding SA-002, blocking; landed under S2-20 and S2-26 rather than as a new §1.2 row, because both directions reduce to states those decisions already enumerate — an immutable version set plus a mutable pointer (S2-20), and a member score on the already-ratified `no_cohort_evidence` path (S2-26). Revision 7 specified the **initial** zero-evidence case — §2.3(a): a cohort whose window holds only eBay observations produces no baseline row at all — and never specified the **reachable reverse boundary**. A cohort that *has* a current version can lose its final price event four ways, all of them ordinary: B2 ages the last run out of the 90-day window; an operator or a sweep deletes the last snapshots (§3.3.4); a source is reclassified into `BOUNDED_RETENTION_CLASSES` and its observations stop reaching the SQL pull (§2.3a); or the last member is re-attributed or re-resolved out of the key (B5). Revision 7 forbade a synthetic empty version, did not say what `current_version` becomes, and gave L1/C2 no NULL case — so an implementer either invents pointer semantics or leaves old scores citing and rendering a baseline whose evidence is entirely gone.

**Definition (normative).** A cohort key is in the **baseline-void** state exactly when its `cohort_baseline_current` row exists and `current_version IS NULL`. The pointer row **survives**; only the FK is cleared.

*Rejected alternative — deleting the pointer row.* It is the smaller schema change and it breaks detection twice. The pointer is the only home of `ingest_high_water`, so deleting it resets the B1 cursor to the beginning of time and the next evaluation re-consumes the whole window; and it is the only home of `last_evaluated_at`, which B4 compares against, so a refdata change during the void interval would be undetectable rather than merely late. B5's union rule (§3.4.1) would still surface the key from the live side, but only while live members exist — and a cohort can be void *with* members, which is the common case. Keeping the row costs one nullable FK and keeps every detection stamp continuous across the void interval, which is the property that makes the reverse transition detectable at all.

*Rejected alternative — a synthetic empty baseline version.* §2.3(a) already rejects it for the initial case and §6 records why: it puts a row summarizing no evidence into the audit path and onto the prune path, and every consumer of `observation_vector`, `q25`/`q50`/`q75` and `source_composition` would need an empty-vector branch anyway. The reverse boundary does not get a different answer from the forward one.

**The `version → NULL` transition (`baseline-void` entry).** When a scheduled evaluation of a cohort whose `current_version` is **not** NULL recomputes `E` and finds it **empty**, one transaction does all of the following and nothing else:

1. sets `current_version = NULL` and advances `last_promoted_at` — the pointer moved, and it moved for the same reason a promotion moves it;
2. rewrites every §3.3.5(b) detection stamp, with these values for the empty case, so the triggers are self-clearing exactly as they are for a non-empty evaluation:
   - **`next_window_exit_at = NULL`** — `min over E of last_obs(e) + 90 d` over an empty `E` has no value. B2 is therefore restated as `current.next_window_exit_at IS NOT NULL AND now() ≥ current.next_window_exit_at`, which is false for a void cohort. That is correct rather than convenient: there is no element left to age out, and the re-entry direction is B1's and B3's job, not B2's;
   - **`price_event_digest` = the digest of the empty projection**, `SHA-256(canonical_json([]))` — a value, never NULL, so B3 is clean on the next tick and fires the instant a first event appears;
   - `member_projection_digest` — computed normally over the live members, which a void cohort may still have;
   - `raw_snapshot_count = 0` and `source_raw_snapshot_counts = {}`; `ingest_high_water` and `last_evaluated_at` advance as on any successful evaluation;
3. **inserts no `cohort_baseline` row.** No version is minted, so the immutability and content-addressing rules are untouched;
4. **rescores every member**, through L1/C2 — see the fan-out below.

**No version row is deleted, and old scores stay exactly as valid as they were.** The version the pointer used to name is immutable and stays immutable; the `PROTECT` FK on `ListingScore.cohort_baseline` means a score that cites it keeps it. What the void state changes is *which* baseline is current, not what any historical score was computed against — and because §3.3.5(d) puts the sufficient evidence on the score row, a historical score's number, percentile, margin and source composition all still regenerate with no baseline access at all. The **prune** interaction is a consequence, not a special case: condition 1 of §3.3.5(f) ("not named by any `current_version`") becomes true for that version, so it becomes prunable once it is past `BASELINE_VERSION_GRACE` **and** condition 2 holds — and condition 2 is enforced by `PROTECT`, so a version any surviving score still cites is not deleted. Exactly the versions nothing cites are reclaimed, which is the intended behavior of an emptied cohort.

**The member fan-out, and why it is not simply "everyone becomes `no_cohort_evidence`".** The void cohort's key is one **rung** of the §3.3.2 ladder, not a listing's final answer. Each member is rescored by re-running the ladder from its own step-0 key, and the outcome is whatever the ladder returns:

- if a relaxed rung (condition-dropped, capacity-widened, or tier-parent) has a current version with usable observations, the member is scored against **that** rung, with `price_basis = 'cohort'`, its own `relaxation_step` and `relaxation_exhausted`, and a **non-NULL** baseline FK — the §3.3.5(c) CHECKs are satisfied without change;
- only when the ladder **terminates with zero usable observations** does the member take S2-7's neutral path with `price_basis = 'no_cohort_evidence'`, `provisional_reasons = ['zero_observations']`, `n_eff = 0`, `λ = 0` as stored zeros, `q` / quartiles / `margin_iqr` NULL, `cohort_key` = the terminal relaxed key and `cohort_baseline_id = NULL` — the representation §3.3.5(c) and §3.4.1 group 4 already fix, reached by a new route rather than defined a second time.

This is the same statement §3.3.2 makes for a cohort that was never populated; the void state adds no fourth `price_basis` value and no new neutral representation. It also means the fan-out of a void transition is **not** confined to that key's members: relaxing pushes those listings onto a broader rung, so the broader rung's own membership is unchanged but its scores now include arrivals. No extra trigger is needed — the arriving listings are the ones L1/C2 selected, and the incumbent members of the broader rung are unaffected because their own `D` did not move.

**The `NULL → version` transition (`baseline-void` exit).** When an evaluation of a cohort whose `current_version` **is** NULL recomputes a **non-empty** `E`, the same transaction inserts (or content-addressed-reuses) exactly one version, promotes the pointer to it, rewrites the detection stamps normally, and rescores every member — which returns those the ladder had relaxed to `price_basis = 'cohort'` against this key. §3.3.5(c) already states this for the eBay-only special case ("add a non-eBay observation to an eBay-only cohort and assert a baseline version appears"); (i) generalizes it to every void cohort, including one that previously held versions. **Content-addressing makes the round trip cheap and exact**: if the returning evidence reproduces a distribution the key has seen before, the existing version is reused and no row is inserted, so an oscillation across the window boundary costs pointer updates and rescores, never version growth.

**Which trigger detects each direction, checked rather than assumed.** Entry is detected by whichever trigger the emptying mechanism moved — **B2** for the window-exit path (the last element's deadline is in the pointer and passes), **B3** for deletion and source reclassification, **B5** for a membership emptying. Exit is detected by **B1** (an eligible snapshot arrived above the high-water mark) and confirmed by **B3**. Two reconciliations are required for that to be true, and both are stated in §3.4.1 rather than left to the reader: **B2 must tolerate a NULL deadline** (above), and **B3 must be evaluated over the union of stored pointer keys and live grouped keys, with the missing side taken as the empty-projection digest** — precisely the rule B5 already carries. Without B3's union, a void cohort produces no group row in the 90-day grouped query and would simply be skipped, so the *entry* direction would depend entirely on B2 and the *exit* direction entirely on B1's cursor. That is the same structural gap SA-023 found in B5, in the trigger that exists to be the backstop.

**Tests — cited elsewhere as §3.3.5(i) tests 1–6, distinct from the section-wide list below** (MS-2c for the ladder outcome, MS-2d for the transitions — SA-002's `suggested_validation`, live DB):

1. **Entry by window exit, exactly as the reviewer framed it.** Create a cohort with **one** usable price event and current `cohort`-basis scores for its members; seed the ladder so relaxation is **exhausted with zero observations** at every rung. Advance the clock past the pointer's `next_window_exit_at`. Assert: B2 fired; `E` is empty; `current_version IS NULL`; `next_window_exit_at IS NULL`; `price_event_digest` equals the empty-projection digest; `raw_snapshot_count = 0`; **zero** `cohort_baseline` rows inserted and the previously current version still present and unmodified; **exactly one** rescore per member, each to `price_basis = 'no_cohort_evidence'` with `cohort_baseline_id = NULL`, `n_eff = 0`, `λ = 0`, `q`/quartiles/`margin_iqr` NULL and `provisional_reasons = ['zero_observations']`.
2. **The quiet tick, then exit.** Run a further refresh with nothing changed and assert **zero** writes anywhere — B2 does not re-fire on the NULL deadline and B3 does not re-fire on the empty digest, which is what makes the void state stable rather than permanently dirty. Then add the first usable event and assert: **exactly one** baseline version is promoted; every member returns to `price_basis = 'cohort'` with a non-NULL FK; and one rescore per member, not two.
3. **Entry with a relaxation escape.** The same emptying, but with a populated tier-parent rung. Assert every member lands on `price_basis = 'cohort'` against the **relaxed** key with the correct `relaxation_step`, that **no** member takes the neutral path, and that the incumbent members of the relaxed rung are **not** rescored (their own `D` did not move).
4. **Entry by deletion, through B3's union.** Delete the cohort's last surviving eligible snapshots without crossing any window boundary. Assert B3 fired via the union rule (the key has a pointer row and no group row), the same terminal pointer state as case 1, and — the negative control that pins the union — that the identical fixture with B2 disabled still detects the emptying.
5. **Prune interaction.** After case 1, age the orphaned version past `BASELINE_VERSION_GRACE`. Assert it is prunable and counted **when no score cites it**, and that when a surviving `merchant_fact` score still cites it the delete raises `ProtectedError`, the version survives, and the sweep continues (§3.3.5f). Assert in both branches that the cited historical score still re-derives byte-identically through §3.4.3 dispatch with no baseline readable.
6. **Round-trip content addressing.** Cross the boundary out and back with the same observation set restored. Assert the exit reuses the **existing** version row rather than inserting a second one, and that `cohort_baseline` row count is unchanged across the whole round trip.

**Tests (MS-2a for the model, MS-2c for the reproduction — SA-015's `suggested_validation`):**

1. **The SA-015 reproduction, exactly as the reviewer framed it.** Compute baseline version `V1` for cohort `K`; score listing `S1` against it. Change cohort membership (add a cheaper member) and refresh `K`, producing `V2` and promoting the pointer. Delete every `offer_snapshot` in the cohort and delete `V2`'s vector from the reader's reach (re-read only the `listing_score` row). Re-derive `S1` from `S1.inputs_json` + `algorithm_version` through §3.4.3 dispatch; assert exact equality of all four subscores, `base_score`, `deal_score`, `explanation_json` **and `explanation_text` byte for byte** (SA-015 round 4: the margin-in-IQR and source-composition clauses are regenerated from group 4's quartiles, `margin_iqr` and `source_composition_rendered`, with **no** baseline row readable); assert `S1.cohort_baseline` still names `V1`; assert `V1.baseline_digest == S1.inputs_json[group 4].baseline_digest`; assert `V1.observation_vector` is byte-identical to what it was at step 1.
2. **Immutability, through every writer rather than through one (SA-003's `suggested_validation`, revision 8).** Persist a released version, then attempt to mutate it through **each** of `Model.save()`, `QuerySet.update()`, `bulk_update()`, a Django admin change POST, and raw SQL executed on the ordinary application connection under the runtime role; assert **every** one is rejected and the row is byte-identical afterwards, and that the raw-SQL and bulk paths are rejected by the **database** (the §3.3.5a trigger) rather than by Python — an assertion on the exception's origin, because a test that only proves "it raised" would pass against the revision-7 mechanism the finding is about. Assert the admin registration reports no add, change or delete permission. **Negative controls, so the trigger is proven not to be over-broad:** an `INSERT` of a new version succeeds; a `DELETE` of an uncited version succeeds (§3.3.5f needs it); and an `UPDATE` of the `cohort_baseline_current` pointer succeeds, since every mutable stamp lives there (§3.3.5b). Separately, assert a second refresh producing the same digest inserts **no** version row and writes only that one pointer row's evaluation stamps (§3.3.5b: `last_evaluated_at`, `ingest_high_water` and the detection stamps, none of whose values may differ in this fixture because nothing was polled and nothing moved) — the same qualified form the §3.4.1 no-drift test asserts, so this test and test 5 below cannot be read as contradicting each other.
3. **Prune.** Age a superseded, uncited version past `BASELINE_VERSION_GRACE` and assert it is pruned and counted; make one `listing_score` row cite it and assert the delete raises `ProtectedError`, the version survives, and the sweep continues; assert the **current** version is never a prune candidate even when uncited.
4. **Measurement.** MS-2c records versions created per cohort per day and bytes per day over the seeded window; MS-2d records the same in the first production week (§4).
5. **The SA-025 digest-identical advance (MS-2d, live DB) — the reviewer's own validation.** Create version `V1` for cohort `K`, then keep polling one member at an **unchanged** price past `V1`'s original `next_window_exit_at`. Assert, over the whole crossing: every tick recomputes a `baseline_digest` equal to `V1`'s and inserts **zero** version rows; the pointer's `next_window_exit_at` **advances** at each evaluation; B2 is clean on the tick after the one that fired; and `K` is not re-evaluated on every subsequent tick. The same fixture against revision 6's placement re-evaluates `K` on every tick indefinitely, which is the regression this test pins.
6. **Provenance-only B5 (MS-2d, live DB).** Change one member's provenance layer with every value byte-identical (the §3.4.1 fifth B5 case). Assert `member_projection_digest` moves, B5 fires **once**, the recomputed `baseline_digest` is unchanged, **zero** version rows are inserted, the pointer's `member_projection_digest` is rewritten, and **B5 is clean on the next tick**. Under revision 6 the cohort stays dirty forever, which is the half of SA-025 that never self-heals.
7. **Source re-attribution (MS-2c/MS-2d, live DB).** Move one member listing to a different **non-eBay** `source_site`, changing no price, capacity or quantity. Assert the `observation_vector` is element-for-element identical, the semantic `source_composition` differs, `baseline_digest` therefore **differs**, exactly one new version is inserted and promoted, the cohort's listings are rescored, and each new score's `source_composition_rendered` matches the new version. **Negative control:** a poll that changes only `raw_snapshot_count` leaves `baseline_digest` and the version count untouched and writes only the pointer.

### MS-2d — Scoring service and the scheduled job

- `ScoringService.refresh()` — the two-phase run of S2-3, wrapped in a **`ScoringRun`** row (§3.4.4) with counts, runtime, and `RunFailureClass` on error, so NFR-004 covers scoring the same way it covers acquisition.
- **Failure isolation:** a cohort that fails to score is logged, flagged, and skipped — it never aborts the refresh for other cohorts, mirroring NFR-001's per-source isolation.

#### 3.4.1 The invalidation model

Review finding SA-003 of audit `9f6079cb`, blocking (not audit `b2eedc33`’s SA-003, which is §3.3.5a immutability enforcement): the round-1 rule ("a cohort is dirty when a snapshot has `observed_at` later than `baseline.computed_at`", plus grain changes and version bumps) invalidates on **insertions only**, so a score could stay current forever while its inputs expired, its window slid, its seller's rating changed, or its refdata was corrected.

The replacement has two halves: **which baselines are stale**, and **which listings must be rescored**.

**A. Baseline staleness.** A cohort's **current baseline version** (§3.3.5) is stale — meaning the cohort must be recomputed, which has exactly **three** outcomes: it promotes a newly inserted (or content-addressed reused) version; or, when the recomputed digest is unchanged, writes **no version row at all**; or — revision 8, review finding SA-002 — when the recomputed `E` is **empty**, clears `current_version` to NULL and enters the §3.3.5(i) **baseline-void** state, which inserts no version either but does fan out to the members. In all three the evaluation rewrites the pointer's evaluation stamps — `last_evaluated_at`, `ingest_high_water` and every §3.3.5(b) detection stamp (`next_window_exit_at`, `price_event_digest`, `member_projection_digest`, `raw_snapshot_count`, `source_raw_snapshot_counts`), recomputed and stored on **every** successful evaluation so a trigger that fired without a semantic change is clean on the next tick (revision 7, review finding SA-025) — when **any** of the following holds:

| # | Trigger | Detection (no queue, per S2-4) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| B1 | **A row was inserted** into the cohort's observation set | `EXISTS(eligible offer_snapshot of a member listing with ingested_at > current.ingest_high_water)`, per §2.2.2. **Not `observed_at`** — that is merchant observation time and a backfilled older row evades it entirely (review finding SA-020). The high-water carries the §2.2.2 commit-lag margin, so an in-flight insert is picked up on a later tick rather than skipped |
| B2 | **A price event left the 90-day window** | `current.next_window_exit_at IS NOT NULL AND now() ≥ current.next_window_exit_at`, the stored `min over E of last_obs(e) + 90 d` (§3.3.4). **The NULL guard is revision 8, review finding SA-002**: a baseline-void cohort has an empty `E` and therefore no deadline, and a void cohort's re-entry is B1's and B3's to detect, never B2's. The deadline stamp is on the **pointer** and is rewritten on every successful evaluation (§3.3.5b, revision 7, review finding SA-025); on the immutable version it could not advance after a digest-identical refresh, so B2 fired on every tick forever. The stored stamp is what makes this detectable **without any new snapshot**, which is the round-1 gap; it replaces revision 4's `oldest_observed_at` test, which the left-censored carry-in of §3.3.4 would make permanently true. This is a real membership change, not a decay effect: an element leaves the vector |
| B3 | **The content of `E` changed at all** — deletion, backfill, run re-bounding | `price_event_digest(E(cohort, window)) ≠ current.price_event_digest` — the stored term is on the **pointer** since revision 7 (§3.3.5b, SA-025) — **both terms are the §3.3.4 price-event projection, hashed by the same rule** (review findings SA-017, SA-020). Revision 4 compared `count(E)`, which cannot see an insert or deletion that leaves the cardinality unchanged; a digest can. `raw_snapshot_count` remains barred from every dirtiness comparison. **Evaluated over the *union* of stored pointer keys and live grouped keys, missing side = the empty-projection digest** — revision 8, review finding SA-002, the same union rule B5 already carries: without it a cohort whose events all disappeared has no group row in the 90-day query and is silently skipped, so the backstop would miss exactly the emptying it exists to catch (§3.3.5i) |
| B4 | Cohort-attribute / refdata change | any `drive_spec`, `product_model`, `product_variant` **or `source_site`** row reachable from a member listing has `updated_at > current.last_evaluated_at` (the first three are `TimeStamped`, verified at `catalog/models/identity.py:126,163,200`; `SourceSite` is `TimeStamped` too, `catalog/models/market.py:39`). Revision 7 adds `source_site` because the semantic `source_composition` is now part of `baseline_digest` and is keyed by `source_site.normalized_name` (§2.3a, SA-025), so a **rename** changes version identity while touching no listing row and no snapshot |
| B5 | **Membership or member-projection change** | the recomputed `member_projection_digest` differs from the **pointer's** (revision 7, SA-025 — on the immutable version it could never be advanced by a digest-identical evaluation, so a provenance-only change left the cohort permanently dirty) — evaluated over the **union** of stored and live cohort keys (below). This catches a same-grain re-resolution target change, a listing moving between cohorts because its **extracted** attributes changed, and a cohort losing its last member (review finding SA-023) |
| B6 | `algorithm_version` bump | `current_version.algorithm_version ≠ ALGORITHM_VERSION` |

Throughout this section, **`current`** is the `cohort_baseline_current` pointer row and **`version`** / `current_version` is the immutable `cohort_baseline` row it names (§3.3.5). Every mutable stamp is on the pointer; every semantic field is on the version — revision 7 makes that true of the schema as well as of the prose, which is review finding SA-025.

**There is no time-only staleness trigger, and revision 2's `BASELINE_MAX_AGE` ceiling is withdrawn (review finding SA-014).** Advancing `now` without any dependency change multiplies every weight by one positive constant: `w_i(now) = 2^(−(now − t_i)/30 d) = 2^(−(now − t₀)/30 d) · 2^((t_i − t₀)/30 d) = c(now) · v_i`, where `v_i` depends only on the observation timestamp. Every statistic this design reads from the weight vector is a ratio of weight sums, so every one of them is invariant under that common factor:

- `n_eff = (Σw)²/Σ(w²)` is exactly scale-invariant (§3.2.3 property 1), so `λ = min(1, n_eff/30)` is too;
- the weighted rank `q` (§3.3.4) and the weighted quantiles behind `margin_iqr` are ratios of weight sums against the same total, so they are invariant as well.

Revision 2's claim that "`n_eff` and `λ` would drift on every tick" was therefore false, and B7 was doing no work except forcing a full cohort recomputation every hour and — because the baseline's row identity and `computed_at` were part of the L2 fingerprint — appending a fresh `listing_score` row for every affected listing on an otherwise quiet system. Baselines now refresh only on B1–B6, all of which are real dependency changes, and the incidental identity of a recomputation is kept out of the decision digest by §3.4.1's `baseline_digest` rule below.

**Two stamps, on two tables — and, since revision 7, every detection stamp with them.** A recomputation that produces the same semantic content must be invisible downstream. Under §3.3.5 the stamps separate cleanly by mutability:

- **`cohort_baseline.computed_at`** — the immutable creation instant of a **version**. Because a version exists only when its `baseline_digest` is new, "the current version's `computed_at` advanced" and "the semantic content changed" are the same statement, which is what L1 reads. Revision 3 achieved this with a conditional `UPDATE`; versioning makes it structural.
- **`cohort_baseline_current.last_evaluated_at`** — a mutable freshness stamp advanced on **every** evaluation, changed or not, on the pointer row rather than on the evidence. B1, B4 and B5 compare against it, so an unchanged cohort is not re-detected as dirty on the next tick. This mirrors `ListingResolution.last_evaluated_at` verbatim, including its rationale: the evidence describes the verdict, the stamp describes when the verdict was last reconfirmed. Advancing it is a one-row update that touches no observation data, so a quiet tick still writes no evidence.
- **The pointer's detection stamps** — `next_window_exit_at` (B2), `price_event_digest` (B3), `member_projection_digest` (B5), and the raw-grain diagnostics `raw_snapshot_count` / `source_raw_snapshot_counts` (§3.3.5b, review finding SA-025). They are recomputed and written on **every** successful evaluation, digest-identical or not, in the same one-row update as `last_evaluated_at`. This is what makes B1–B6 self-clearing: a trigger that fires without a semantic change costs one evaluation and one row update, never a permanent dirty state. Revision 6 kept these on the immutable version, where a reused version could not advance them at all.

**`baseline_digest`** is `SHA-256` over the canonical JSON (§3.4.2 canonicalization) of the **semantic** baseline only: `cohort_key`, `relaxation_step`, `relaxation_exhausted`, `window_days`, `half_life_days`, `algorithm_version`, the §3.3.4 `observation_vector`, and `price_event_count` (which is `len(observation_vector)`, so it adds no freedom — it is included so a truncated vector cannot silently share a digest with a complete one), and — new in revision 7, review finding SA-025 — the **semantic `source_composition`**: the per-source `{normalized_name, price_event_count, weight_sum}` list of §2.3(a), ordered by `normalized_name`. Including it makes source attribution part of version identity, so the composition evidence a score renders (§3.3.5d) can never be stale on a reused version; it costs nothing at poll cadence because both of its terms are price-event-grain quantities, constant between price events for exactly the reason `w_rel` is. The digest contains no row id, no `computed_at`, no `last_evaluated_at`, no `ingest_high_water`, no `next_window_exit_at`, no `price_event_digest`, no `member_projection_digest` and **not** `raw_snapshot_count` or the per-source raw counts — all of which move without the evidence moving, and all of which live on the pointer (§3.3.5b). Because the observation vector stores each element's weight **anchored to the newest price event** rather than to `now` or to the newest raw snapshot (§3.3.4, S2-25), and because an element's `event_at` is a historical run start rather than a window-relative position, the digest is constant across ticks for a fixed observation set — which is what makes "quiet system, zero writes" true rather than aspirational. Revision 4's `max(observed_at)` anchor did not have that property: it advanced on every poll of every member, so every element's `w_rel` moved and the digest with it.

If a future version introduces a statistic that is genuinely not scale-invariant — an absolute weight sum, a calendar-anchored decay, or an absolute-currency threshold — a time trigger has to come back with it. That is the condition that reopens this decision, and it is recorded here so a later author does not reintroduce B7 by reflex.

**B. Listing rescore triggers.** A listing in `P` (§2.2) is rescored when **any** of the following holds:

| # | Trigger | Reaches |
| --- | --- | --- |
| L1 | Its cohort's current baseline **version** changed (`current.current_version_id IS DISTINCT FROM the version the score cites`) | Every listing in that cohort. **The comparison is `IS DISTINCT FROM`, not `≠`** — revision 8, review finding SA-002: both operands are nullable (the pointer's FK is NULL in the §3.3.5(i) baseline-void state; the score's FK is NULL on every §3.3.5(c) neutral path), and SQL `≠` is NULL-valued rather than true on either side, so a `≠` reading would silently skip both the `version → NULL` and the `NULL → version` transitions — the exact two directions SA-002 named |
| L2 | Its **canonical input fingerprint** changed (below) | That listing — reached by candidate clause **C3** for facts on the `Listing` row and by **C9** for the §2.2.1 current-offer facts of group 1 (revision 8, review finding SA-001) |
| L3 | Its `resolution_grain` changed, or its current `listing_resolution` id changed at the same grain | That listing (and, via B5, its old and new cohorts) |
| L4 | A `SellerRatingObservation` was written for its seller | Every listing in `P` for that seller — **including the first row that seller has ever had**, which is the transition revision 10's C5 could not detect (revision 11, review finding SA-008). The two directions of the S2-12 prior are now both covered and they are covered by different clauses: **prior → first row is C5's `COALESCE` branch**; **row → prior (the last row deleted) is L9 + C1**, because C5 can only ever look forwards |
| L5 | `SourceConfig.seller_policy_prior` changed for its source | Every listing in `P` for that source |
| L6 | It re-entered `P` (relist, or a snapshot pushing `expires_at` forward) | That listing — it has no current decision digest, so §3.4.2 always writes |
| L7 | `algorithm_version` bump | The entire population (a full rescore, S2-6) |
| L8 | The snapshot `S(listing)` names was deleted or expired, so §2.2.1's selector re-evaluates | That listing — driven from the sweep's own deleted keys, not from a re-read, because the current facts move *backwards* in time (§2.2.1). **Its guard and the cross-job protocol that makes it hold under concurrency are §3.4.2's serialization contract** (revision 9, review finding SA-006): without it a concurrent refresh can overwrite this invalidation with a decision derived from the deleted row |
| L9 | **The selected `SellerRatingObservation` was deleted or expired**, so §3.2.5(d)'s selection re-evaluates | Every listing in `P` for that seller — driven from the sweep's deleted keys for the same reason as L8: the seller aggregate moves *backwards* to an older surviving row, or to the S2-12 prior, and no fingerprint re-read is scheduled to notice (review finding SA-022) |

L4 and L5 are covered mechanically by L2 once the seller aggregate and the prior are part of the fingerprint; they are listed separately because the **fan-out** differs and the MS-2d test must exercise each fan-out shape independently.

**L9 and its sweep hook (review finding SA-022).** `SellerRatingObservation` is `RetentionGoverned` and eBay-sourced rows carry `ebay_listing_observation`, so the sweep deletes them on the ordinary six-hour clock — and `purge_expired` deletes every expired bounded model **in independently selected batches and clears nothing on any parent** (verified at `catalog/management/commands/purge_expired.py:186-223`). L4 fires only on a *write*, so before revision 5 a listing could keep a seller trust value, a cap outcome and an explanation clause derived from a rating row that no longer existed.

- **The hook.** `SellerRatingObservation.invalidate_seller_scores(deleted_keys)` — a classmethod discovered and dispatched by the sweep exactly the way it already discovers `deletion_exempt_q` and `redact_expired` (`purge_expired.py:112-168`, `getattr(model, ...)`), called **inside the same per-model transaction as the delete**, taking the deleted `(seller_id, observed_at)` pairs. It is the seller-side sibling of `ListingScore.clear_parent_denorm(deleted_keys)` from §2.3(b), and `OfferSnapshot.invalidate_listing_scores(deleted_keys)` is the L8 sibling of both; MS-2a lands all three under one hook protocol so the sweep gains no per-model special cases.
- **What it does — no queue, per S2-4.** It clears the `current_*` columns (§2.1) of every listing in `P` for the affected sellers. **That membership query bounds the clear; it does not define it** (revision 11, review finding SA-009): the set that must be cleared is "listings whose current score cites a deleted rating", a stored fact, and §3.4.2's `seller` anchor is what makes the two coincide by stopping any listing outside the query from acquiring such a score in the first place. A listing with no `current_scored_at` has no current decision digest, so §3.4.2 always writes for it and the refresh's candidate set picks it up on the next tick (the same mechanism L6 already relies on for a relisted listing). No `score_recompute_queue` table is introduced, which is what S2-4 rules out.
- **The clear is *one-time*, and revision 10 stops treating that as sufficient on its own (review finding SA-007 of audit `b2eedc33`, blocking).** "Picked up on the next tick" holds only while nothing puts `current_scored_at` back. A refresh that computed its decision from the rating this hook just deleted, and then commits, does exactly that — and there is no second detection path behind it, because **C5 looks only for an observation *newer* than the one the score recorded** while a deletion moves the aggregate *backwards*. L9 is therefore not self-healing the way B1–B6 are, and its guarantee is completed by §3.4.2's commit-time revalidation: the selected seller observation and its normalized aggregate are members of `D_selected`, so such a refresh abandons its write instead of overwriting this clear. The two statements are one guarantee written from both ends, and neither half is sufficient alone.
- **Conditional, like §2.3(b)'s clear.** The clear applies only when the deleted pair **is** the seller's currently selected observation — a batch that deletes an older, unselected row changes `aggregate(seller)` not at all and must not cost a fan-out. The guard is the same shape as the `AND current_scored_at = %(scored_at)s` predicate: clear only where the score's stored group-3 `observed_at` equals a deleted row's. **This guard is *cross-row* where §2.3(b)'s is not** — it reads the score row while updating the listing row — so it is only sound under §3.4.2's serialization contract, which makes the hook take the parent `listing` lock before evaluating it (revision 9, review finding SA-006, found while closing that finding rather than raised separately).
- **The post-delete aggregate is §3.2.5(d) re-run over the survivors**: the greatest-`observed_at` surviving row **whatever its usability**, and the S2-12 source prior when none survives. There is no separate rule here — that is the point of making selection unconditional in SA-016.

**Tests (MS-2d, live DB — SA-022's `suggested_validation`):** score five listings of one eBay seller against a usable rating; expire that rating; run `purge_expired`; assert **every** one of the five is rescored **exactly once**, that the new aggregate is the next surviving row (in one case) or the S2-12 prior with `seller_no_rating` (in a second case where no row survives), and that the seller's cap outcome and explanation change accordingly. Negative controls: deleting a **non-selected** older row rescores **nothing**; a second sweep with nothing to delete writes nothing; a seller with no listings in `P` produces no fan-out.

**The canonical dependency document.** Review finding SA-003 of audit `9f6079cb`, second half: revision 2 enumerated *derived* values (normalized quantity, capacity, bases) in the fingerprint, so an extraction change that left every normalized value alone — a corrected `source_text`, a re-tuned confidence, a different producing layer — did not have to trigger recomputation, and the current row kept explaining itself with evidence that no longer existed. The fix is to stop maintaining two lists.

`D(listing)` is **one canonical dependency document**: the complete, ordered set of persisted facts the decision consumes, each carried **with its provenance record rather than only its normalized value**. It has exactly four groups:

1. **Offer facts** — taken from **`S(listing)`, the §2.2.1 current offer snapshot**, and from no other row: `usd_total_landed_price`, `total_landed_price`, source currency, `fx_rate` and `stock_status`. Revision 3 said "the snapshot they came from" without saying which one; §2.2.1 (decision S2-22) is now the rule, and because it selects by greatest `observed_at` among eligible rows, a late-arriving older snapshot changes nothing in this group (review finding SA-018).

   **When `S(listing)` does not exist, group 1 is still total — revision 9, review finding SA-005.** The `price_unavailable` path (§2.2.1) selects no row, and the mandatory availability subscore still has to read a `stock_status`. Group 1 therefore has **one** canonical payload on that path, and it reads nothing from any snapshot:

   | Group-1 key | Value when `S(listing)` is absent | Why |
   | --- | --- | --- |
   | `usd_total_landed_price` | JSON `null` under the explicit key | No eligible row exists; the key is present so canonicalization and `inputs_digest` stay well defined (the §3.4.1 group-4 rule, applied here) |
   | `total_landed_price` | JSON `null` | Same |
   | source currency | JSON `null` | Same |
   | `fx_rate` | JSON `null` | It is the eligibility predicate itself; a value here would contradict `price_unavailable` |
   | `stock_status` | The literal string `"unknown"` — the existing `StockStatus.UNKNOWN` member (`catalog/models/market.py:57-61`) | §3.2.4's availability table is exhaustive over the four enum values **with no fall-through**, and this is the cell whose meaning — "we have no established stock state" — is exactly true here. It is an **asserted** value, not one read from an unselected row, which is what keeps §2.2.1's no-field-mixing rule intact |

   `s_avail` is then `0.50` with the `stock_unknown` flag by §3.2.4, alongside `fx_unavailable`; `ListingScore.offer_observed_at` is NULL and `Listing.current_offer_observed_at` is NULL, so C9's `COALESCE` branch and C1 both remain able to select the listing. **A consequence worth stating because L8 depends on it:** a `price_unavailable` row consumed **no** snapshot fact, so no snapshot deletion can make it stale — which is why L8's guard comparing against a NULL `current_offer_observed_at` never fires for such a listing and that is correct rather than a gap.

   **The selected snapshot's own `observed_at` is *not* a member of this group** — revision 8, review finding SA-001. Revisions 3–7 listed it here, and it is the only term in `D` that moves on a poll which changes nothing a score reads. It was inert only because the SA-001 gap kept a re-polled listing out of the candidate set; candidate clause C9 removes that gap, so the term would now append one `listing_score` row per listing per poll. It is removed on the rule this section already states rather than as an exception to it: `observed_at` is consumed by the **selector**, not by the decision — no subscore, veto, cap, risk flag or explanation clause reads it — so `D`, which carries every fact the decision *consumed*, never had a claim to it. It is retained as `ListingScore.offer_observed_at`, a **provenance output column** on the row (§2.1), excluded from `inputs_json`, `inputs_digest` and `decision_digest` (§3.4.2), so "which observation was this decision taken against" stays answerable and auditable while ceasing to be a write trigger. §2.2.1's C9 test 7 is the regression guard.
2. **Extraction provenance** — the full `Listing.extracted_attrs_json` contents verbatim: for **every** N2 attribute present, the complete `{value, confidence, layer, source_text}` record from `matching/types.py` `Attribute[T]`, not the value alone. This is the group revision 2 was missing, and it is why a provenance-only change now produces a new decision.
3. **Authoritative catalog and seller facts** — `capacity_tb_per_unit`, `condition`, `recert_channel`, `packaging`, `returns_policy` + `returns_window_days`, `recording_tech` (including its NULL state), `market_tier`, canonical `interface`, canonical `form_factor`, `media_type`, `dwpd`, `warranty_channel` + `warranty_months`, the **§3.2.5 seller aggregate** as of scoring — the normalized `p_obs`, `n`, `y`, `count_basis`, `is_usable` and the `observed_at` of the single selected `SellerRatingObservation` (never a sum, never an average; revision 3's `positive_count` / `total_count` pair is withdrawn with the model fields, review finding SA-016) — `seller_policy_prior`, and the current `listing_resolution` id + `grain` + `matcher_version`.
4. **The baseline evidence snapshot** (review finding SA-015, renamed in revision 5) — the cohort context plus everything the price subscore **and its explanation** were computed from: `cohort_key`, `relaxation_step`, `relaxation_exhausted`, the baseline's **`baseline_digest`**, and **`q`**, **`n_eff`**, **`lambda_shrinkage`**, this listing's own **`ln_price_per_tb`** and **`w_rel`**, its **`in_baseline`** membership flag, the weighted **`q25` / `q50` / `q75`**, **`margin_iqr`**, and **`source_composition_rendered`** (§3.3.5d gives the field-by-field justification). Deliberately **not** the baseline row id and **not** its `computed_at`: those are recomputation identity, not semantics, and including them is exactly what made revision 2's hourly refresh append a row per listing (SA-014).

   The snapshot is what makes a historical score re-derivable after its cohort has moved on — §3.3.5(d) states why a digest alone is not evidence and what the reproduction test asserts. Its members are **inputs to this row's decision**, recorded as such, not derived outputs: `q` and the quartiles cannot be recomputed from anything else the row stores once the baseline version's vector is out of reach, which is precisely the state the reviewer's test creates. Their presence in `D` also means a cohort change that moves this listing's `q` or its margin moves its `inputs_digest` directly, rather than only through `baseline_digest`.

   On a neutral path (§3.3.5c) the cohort consumed no evidence, so the snapshot degenerates rather than disappearing. Revision 6 said the degenerate snapshot made `n_eff` and `λ` **NULL** here while S2-7, §3.2.6, §3.3.5(c) and the neutral-path tests all required `n_eff = 0` and `λ = 0` — review finding SA-027, and the reason MS-2a could not choose a column nullability. **One representation, normative for the whole document, and it is the ratified one:**

   | Group-4 field | Neutral value | Why this and not the other |
   | --- | --- | --- |
   | `n_eff` | `0` — a stored zero, never NULL | S2-7 and §3.2.6 ratified it, and `is_provisional := n_eff < 30` is only a total expression if `n_eff` always has a value |
   | `lambda_shrinkage` (`λ`) | `0` | S2-7 fixes it; `s_price = λ·(1 − q) + (1 − λ)·0.5` then evaluates to exactly `0.5` **without reading `q`** |
   | `q` | NULL | A weighted rank in an empty distribution does not exist. The `λ = 0` factor is what keeps the NULL unreachable instead of propagating into the subscore |
   | `q25`, `q50`, `q75` | NULL | §3.3.4's inverse weighted CDF is total over a **non-empty** vector, and on these paths there is no vector at all |
   | `margin_iqr` | NULL | Its divisor does not exist. This is a **different** NULL from §3.3.4's degenerate-IQR NULL, and `price_basis` is what distinguishes them for a reader and for MS-3 |
   | `ln_price_per_tb` (this listing's own) | The real value for `no_cohort_evidence`; NULL for `capacity_unavailable` and `price_unavailable` | The listing genuinely has a `$/TB` only when it has both a comparable price and a usable capacity |
   | `w_rel` (this listing's own) | NULL | It has no weight share in a vector that does not exist |
   | `in_baseline` | `false` | A recorded fact, not an absent one; the explanation renders it |
   | `baseline_digest` | NULL | Pairs with the §3.3.5(c) NULL-FK CHECK, so the row and its constraint say the same thing |
   | `source_composition_rendered` | `[]` — an empty list, not NULL | An empty composition is a fact the explanation renders as "no comparable cohort evidence" |
   | `cohort_key`, `relaxation_step`, `relaxation_exhausted` | For `price_unavailable` and `no_cohort_evidence`, the terminal relaxed values — both paths run the §3.3.2 ladder. For `capacity_unavailable` the ladder never runs at all, so `cohort_key` and `relaxation_step` are NULL and `relaxation_exhausted` is `false` | §3.3.1 gives a listing with no usable capacity no bucket, so there is nothing to relax; §3.3.5(c)'s second CHECK is the normative statement for `cohort_key` |
   | `price_basis` | Which of the three neutral paths applied | It is what tells a consumer which NULLs to expect |

   "No evidence" is recorded as a **value** wherever a value exists and as JSON `null` under an **explicit key** wherever it does not, so the canonicalization is stable, `inputs_digest` is well defined, and the explanation regenerates on those rows too. §3 (MS-2a) carries the matching database nullability: `n_eff` and `lambda_shrinkage` are NOT NULL columns on every path.

`ListingScore.inputs_digest = SHA-256(canonical_json(D))` under the §3.4.2 canonicalization, and **`ListingScore.inputs_json` is `D` itself** — the row stores the exact document it was digested from, so NFR-007 reproduction reads one field rather than reassembling inputs from a paraphrase. Derived values (`dollars_per_tb`, `quantity`, `quantity_basis`, `price_basis`) are outputs recorded on the row and covered by §3.4.2's decision digest; they are not members of `D`, because digesting both a fact and its own derivation double-counts the fact and hides which one actually moved.

**The one deliberate exception, and the line it draws.** Group 4's values *are* computed rather than read from a row, so they look like the derived outputs the rule above excludes. **Naming the group the "baseline evidence snapshot" is the resolution, not a euphemism**: the distinction is **where the fact they summarize lives**. `dollars_per_tb` is derived from facts `D` already carries in full (price, capacity, quantity), so storing it too would double-count them; every group-4 value summarizes the baseline **version's observation vector**, which `D` deliberately does *not* carry — group 4 holds only its digest. Without them the vector is the fact and the score retains none of it, which is exactly the SA-015 gap, in both its round-3 form (the number) and its round-4 form (the explanation). The rule therefore stands, stated once for the whole document:

> `D` carries every fact the decision consumed. Where the underlying fact is itself retained, `D` carries the fact and never its derivation. Where the underlying fact is **not** retained — today, only the baseline version's observation vector — `D` carries the retained projection of it, and that projection is group 4.

`price_basis` stays an output, because every fact that determines it (capacity presence, snapshot eligibility, cohort emptiness) is already in `D`. `margin_iqr` is in group 4 rather than being an output for the opposite reason: the quartiles it divides by are not otherwise retained anywhere the score can reach.

Because group 4 carries `baseline_digest`, L1 is implied by L2 — but the refresh evaluates L1 first as a set operation, so a semantically changed baseline rescores its cohort in one query rather than by fingerprinting every listing individually. Conversely, a baseline recomputation that produces the same `baseline_digest` changes no listing's `D`, so it writes nothing anywhere.

**`member_projection_digest`** — the B5 stamp, stored on the **`cohort_baseline_current` pointer** since revision 7 (§3.3.5b; revision 6 kept it on the immutable version, where a digest-identical evaluation could never advance it, so a provenance-only change left the cohort permanently dirty — review finding SA-025). Revision 4's `member_resolution_digest` is **withdrawn** (review finding SA-023). Revision 4 hashed `[listing_id, current listing_resolution id]` pairs, which is blind to everything below model grain: §3.3.3 makes **extracted** attributes decide capacity bucket, interface/form, condition and `$/TB` for an unresolved listing, so those values can change — a re-tuned extractor, a corrected title, a new quantity reading — while the resolution id stays identical, moving the listing's observations between cohorts with neither cohort marked stale. The digest is therefore taken over the **cohort-shaping projection** of each member:

```text
π(listing) := [ listing_id,
                current listing_resolution id, resolution grain,
                capacity_tb_per_unit, interface, form_factor,      -- canonical tokens (§3.3.1)
                market_tier, condition, recert_channel,
                quantity, quantity_basis,                          -- §3.2.2
                source_site.normalized_name,                       -- §2.3a composition identity (SA-025)
                provenance layer of each of the above ]            -- authoritative vs. extracted (§3.3.3)
member_projection_digest(K) := SHA-256(canonical_json(sorted by listing_id [ π(l) for l in members(K) ]))
```

- **The projection carries provenance, not just values**, for the same reason group 2 of `D` does (SA-003): a value that stays the same while its layer changes is a different fact about the cohort, and it is exactly the case a value-only digest hides.
- **Source attribution is in the projection** (revision 7, review finding SA-025). The semantic `source_composition` is part of `baseline_digest`, so a listing re-attributed to a different `source_site` changes version identity even when every numeric value is unchanged; B5 is the trigger that has to notice, and a projection without the source could not. A `SourceSite` **rename** touches no listing row, so B4 carries that case instead (`SourceSite` is `TimeStamped`, `catalog/models/market.py:39`; `Listing.source_site` is the FK it is reached through, `catalog/models/market.py:240`).
- **Both the old and the new cohort are dirtied, structurally.** B5 is evaluated over the **union** of (i) every `cohort_baseline_current.cohort_key` and (ii) every cohort key present in the freshly computed grouped projection, with the missing side taken as the **empty digest**. A listing that moves from `K_old` to `K_new` therefore changes `K_old`'s digest (it lost a member — and if it was the last member, `K_old` compares against the empty projection rather than being skipped for having no group row) **and** `K_new`'s digest (gained, or newly created). No per-listing history is needed to reach the old cohort, which is what makes this total rather than best-effort.
- **How the old key is known when it is needed by name.** The grouped union above is the mechanism; `Listing.current_cohort_key` (§2.1) is the *recorded* last-scored key, and it is what the refresh logs, what `detail_json` reports for a moved listing, and what an operator's targeted `manage.py score_refresh --force-cohort <key>` takes. §2.3(b) states why clearing it does not weaken the union rule. The latest `ListingScore.cohort_key` is deliberately **not** the source of truth for this: eBay score rows expire under S2-2a, so that history is not guaranteed to exist.
- **Detection versus authority, unchanged.** B5 stays a **cheap detection shortcut evaluated before the 90-day pull** and is deliberately not the authority on whether to write: `baseline_digest` is that authority, and a B5 hit whose recomputed `baseline_digest` matches produces no **version** write and only rewrites the pointer's evaluation stamps — `member_projection_digest` among them, which is what makes an over-fire cost exactly one row update instead of the permanent dirty state revision 6's placement produced (SA-025). Keeping the two separate is what lets detection be conservative (over-fire freely) without costing a write — which matters more now, because the projection changes more often than a resolution id does.

**Tests (MS-2d, live DB — SA-023's `suggested_validation`):** for a **below-model-grain** listing whose resolution id is held constant, change **only** extracted `capacity`, then only `interface`, then only `condition`, then only `quantity` — four separate cases — and in each assert that the **old** and the **new** cohort's `member_projection_digest` both change, that both baselines are refreshed, that exactly the member listings of both cohorts are rescored, and that no third cohort is touched. A fifth case changes only a provenance layer with every value identical and asserts the digest still moves; a sixth moves the last member out of a cohort and asserts the emptied cohort is compared against the empty projection and refreshed rather than silently skipped. Every case additionally asserts that the **pointer's** `member_projection_digest` was rewritten and that B5 is clean on the following tick (§3.3.5 test 6, SA-025).

**Delist, expiry, and redaction are *not* rescore triggers.** A listing leaving `P` is not rescored; its denormalized fields are cleared per §2.3(b) and its history is left to its retention class.

**C. How the refresh actually finds the affected listings.** The triggers above say *when* a listing must be rescored; this says how a 60 s tick computes that set without a queue (S2-4) and without fingerprinting the whole population. Each clause is one indexed query, and the union is the tick's candidate set:

| # | Candidate clause | Covers |
| --- | --- | --- |
| C1 | `current_scored_at IS NULL` for a listing in `P` | L6, **L8**, **L9**, and every §2.3(b) clear — a listing with no current decision digest is always written by §3.4.2, which is why the sweep hooks clear rather than enqueue |
| C2 | member of a cohort whose `current_version` moved this tick, **"moved" including promotion to NULL and from NULL** (§3.3.5i) | L1, as one set operation after phase 1. Membership for this clause is taken from the cohort's live member set — the same grouped projection B5 computes — so a void cohort's members are still reachable when the pointer names no version (revision 8, review finding SA-002) |
| C3 | `listing.scoring_inputs_changed_at > listing.current_scored_at` | L2 over listing-level facts — `extracted_attrs_json`, `returns_policy`, `returns_window_days` (§2.1 adds the stamp because `Listing` carries **no** `updated_at`) |
| C4 | a reachable `drive_spec` / `product_model` / `product_variant` has `updated_at > listing.current_scored_at` | L2 over group-3 catalog facts — the same join B4 uses, at listing grain |
| **C5** | `listing.seller_id IS NOT NULL AND EXISTS( r ∈ seller_rating_observation(listing.seller) : r.observed_at > COALESCE(score.group3_seller_observed_at, −∞) )` — **the `COALESCE` is revision 11, review finding SA-008**, and it is the same shape C9 already carries. Revision 10 wrote this clause as a bare `>` against the group-3 `observed_at`, which is **NULL on the S2-12 no-row prior** (§3.2.5d), and SQL `NULL > x` is unknown rather than true — so an already-scored seller sitting on the prior would never be selected by its **first** genuine rating. The `COALESCE` branch makes the prior → first-row transition fire, and the clause stays **self-clearing** because §3.2.5(d) selects the latest observation *regardless of usability* (SA-016), so even a first *unusable* row is recorded in group 3 and stops the clause | L4 — **and only L4.** The clause is deliberately one-directional: it detects a *newer* observation and can never detect a *deleted* one, because a deletion moves the aggregate backwards to an older row, an unusable row, or the S2-12 prior, none of which is "greater than" anything. That direction is **L9 + C1**, and §3.4.2's `D_selected` revalidation is what stops a concurrent refresh from undoing it (revision 10, review finding SA-007) |
| C6 | its `SourceConfig.updated_at > listing.current_scored_at` | L5 |
| C7 | its current `listing_resolution` id or grain differs from group 3 | L3 |
| C8 | `ALGORITHM_VERSION ≠` the score's `algorithm_version` | L7 |
| **C9** | `EXISTS(eligible offer_snapshot of this listing with observed_at > COALESCE(listing.current_offer_observed_at, −∞))` | **L2 over group-1 offer facts** — the listing's §2.2.1 current offer snapshot moved. Added in revision 8, review finding SA-001; the clause, its `COALESCE` totality, its self-clearing property, what it must not fire on and the rejected writer-side stamp are all in §2.2.1 |

**L2 has two candidate clauses, not one, and that split is the SA-001 fix.** `D`'s listing-level facts live in two places with two different change mechanisms, so one clause cannot cover both. Group 2 and the group-3 facts stored **on the `Listing` row** are covered by **C3**, which reads the `scoring_inputs_changed_at` stamp `Listing.save()` maintains over `SCORING_INPUT_FIELDS` (§2.1). Group 1 is taken from `S(listing)`, a **child** row that `append_snapshot` writes without touching `Listing` at all (`acquisition/persist.py:74-102`), so no stamp moves and C3 is structurally blind to it; **C9** covers it by comparing stored against live. Revisions 4–7 named L2 as the trigger for a selected-offer insert in the coverage table below and never named the clause that would select the listing, which is why the gap survived four rounds: the trigger was right and unreachable. The general rule this instance is an example of, stated so a later author applies it to a new dependency: **a `D` member that lives on a row other than `Listing` needs its own candidate clause, because `scoring_inputs_changed_at` can only ever describe `Listing` writes.** C4, C5, C6 and C7 exist for exactly the same reason, one per off-`Listing` dependency.

L2's fingerprint is then recomputed **only** for candidates, and §3.4.2 decides whether a row is actually written — so over-firing costs computation and never storage. Two asymmetries are deliberate and are the reason C5 may use `observed_at` while B1 may not: the **seller aggregate consumes exactly one row, selected by greatest `observed_at`**, so a late-arriving older row cannot change it; a **baseline consumes every row in its window**, so a late-arriving older row does change it and needs the §2.2.2 ingestion stamp. The same argument makes §2.2.1's `observed_at` selector sound for group 1.

**Cost, and the one knob if it is too slow.** B1, B2, B4, B5 and C1–C9 are indexed lookups. **B3 is a scan**: one grouped query per refresh computing `price_event_digest` per cohort over the 90-day eligible snapshot set (hypertable chunk exclusion bounds it to the window's chunks). It is the correctness backstop, not the cheap path. MS-2d measures it against the S2-3 60 s budget; if it dominates, the named tunable is `B3_INTERVAL` (a constant under S2-6, proposed **5 minutes**) so B3 runs on a subset of ticks while the indexed triggers run on all of them — a bounded detection *lag*, never a missed change, because a digest mismatch does not expire. Lowering B1's coverage is **not** an available tunable.

##### Dependency × mutation coverage

Every persisted input to `D` and every input to a baseline, against the four ways stored data changes. This table is the completeness argument for §3.4.1: a cell reading "—" must say **why** no trigger is needed, never "not applicable" by omission.

**Revision 11 re-runs the pass with *fan-out membership* treated as a persisted input in its own right** (review finding SA-009's fourth `next_focus` item). The re-run found exactly one input whose membership is a mutable predicate rather than a fixed edge — the seller fan-out — and it is now a row of this table rather than a property assumed by §3.4.2. The result, stated as a rule so a later dependency is checked the same way: **a `D` term whose affected set is a predicate has two obligations, not one — a trigger for each way the *set* changes, and an anchor row that a deleter and a consumer both lock so that a membership change cannot outrun the deletion.** The offer terms need only the first, because an `offer_snapshot`'s affected set is one FK parent and cannot change at all. Two membership mutations turned out to be covered by clauses that already existed (`C3` for reassignment in both directions, §2.3(b) for leaving `P`), and one — `P` entry — is covered by no trigger and needs none, for the reason the new row states. Verified against the trigger definitions above and, for the "no update path" cells, against `acquisition/persist.py:74-102` (append-only) and the model definitions in `catalog/models/market.py`.

| Persisted input | Consumed by | Insert | Update | Delete / expiry | Backfill / late arrival |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `offer_snapshot` — the selected `S(listing)` | `D` group 1 | **L2 via candidate clause C9** (a newer eligible row becomes `S`). Revisions 4–7 named L2 here with **no** candidate clause, so the listing was never selected and the trigger could not run — review finding SA-001, closed in revision 8 (§2.2.1, §3.4.1C). An **ineligible** (NULL-`fx_rate`) insert is deliberately **not** covered and needs no trigger: it cannot become `S`, and no group-1 fact is read from it on any path, including `price_unavailable` (revision 9, SA-005 of `b2eedc33`) | *No update path exists* — the pipeline only appends; a merchant correction is a new row (§2.2.1) | **L8** + `OfferSnapshot.invalidate_listing_scores`, under §3.4.2's serialization contract — the sweep holds the parent `listing` lock and the refresh re-validates the selection under it, so the invalidation cannot be overwritten by a concurrent refresh (revision 9, SA-006 of `b2eedc33`) | *Cannot change `S` by construction* (§2.2.1 selects by greatest `observed_at`); it still reaches the baseline below |
| `offer_snapshot` — the cohort's window set / `E` | `cohort_baseline` | **B1** (`ingested_at` high-water) → confirmed by **B3** | *No update path* | **B3** (digest, over the stored-∪-live key union); **B2** when a run's last observation ages out. When the deletion or the exit removes the **last** element, the evaluation enters the §3.3.5(i) baseline-void state instead of promoting a version, and fans out to the members through L1/C2 (revision 8, SA-002) | **B1 + B3** — the SA-020 case; `observed_at` cannot detect it |
| `listing.extracted_attrs_json` | `D` group 2; cohort key | **L2** (C3) + **B5** (projection) | **L2** (C3) + **B5** | Redaction writes `{}`, so it is an update: **L2 + B5** | Resolver re-write: **L2 + B5**, both cohorts (SA-023) |
| `listing.returns_policy`, `returns_window_days`, `seller`, `source_site` | `D` group 3 (and, through `seller` / `source_site`, which aggregate and which prior apply) | **L2** (C3) | **L2** (C3) — all four are in `SCORING_INPUT_FIELDS`, so any write advances the stamp | Redaction writes the column: **L2** | MS-2e per-source population: **L2** (C3) |
| `listing_resolution` (current edge) | `D` group 3; membership | **L3** (C7) + **B5** | Supersession is an insert plus an `is_current` move: **L3 + B5** | **B5** recomputes from live rows; **L3** compares the stored id | **L3 + B5** |
| `listing.source_site` and `source_site.normalized_name` **as baseline provenance** | `cohort_baseline.source_composition`, and through §3.3.5(d) the rendered explanation | A new listing enters an existing cohort: **B5** (the projection carries the source, SA-025) | Re-attribution of a listing: **B5**; a `SourceSite` rename: **B4** (`TimeStamped`, `catalog/models/market.py:39`) | Deleting a `SourceSite` is blocked by `PROTECT` on `Listing.source_site` (`catalog/models/market.py:240`), so no orphan composition key can arise | A backfilled re-attribution is still an update to the listing row: **B5** |
| `drive_spec` / `product_model` / `product_variant` | `D` group 3; cohort key | Reaches a listing only through a resolution edge: **L3 + B5** | **B4** + **L2** (C4) | `merchant_fact`, never swept — MS-2a asserts the class. An **operator** deletion is caught only when it invalidates the resolution edge (**L3/B5**); otherwise it needs `score_refresh --force-cohort` (§6) | Catalog re-seed moves `updated_at`: **B4 + L2** |
| `seller_rating_observation` (the selected row) | `D` group 3 | **L4** (C5) — **and C5 is total in the prior → first-row direction only since revision 11**: its comparand is NULL on the S2-12 no-row prior, so the clause carries a `COALESCE` (review finding SA-008 of `b2eedc33`). A listing whose `seller_id` is NULL has no seller aggregate to move; it acquires one by a `Listing` write, which is **L2 (C3)** because `seller` is in `SCORING_INPUT_FIELDS` (§2.1) | *No update path* — append-only with the §3.2.5(d) dedupe | **L9** + `SellerRatingObservation.invalidate_seller_scores`, under §3.4.2's serialization contract, whose two locks do **different** jobs and are named separately here since revision 12 (SA-009 of `b2eedc33`): the deleter takes the **`seller` anchor `FOR UPDATE`** and the refresh takes it `FOR SHARE` before selecting the aggregate — **that is the correctness mechanism**, because the affected-listing set is a mutable predicate and a row lock cannot lock a row that does not exist yet — while the **listing fan-out locks are support** for L9's cross-row guard, evaluated outside the READ COMMITTED re-check. The refresh additionally revalidates the **seller** term of `D_selected`, so the one-time L9 clear cannot be overwritten by a refresh computed from the deleted row. C5 is **not** a backstop here: it detects only a newer observation (revision 10, SA-007 of `b2eedc33`) | *Cannot change the aggregate* — selection is greatest `observed_at` (§3.2.5d), so an older late row is not evidence about now |
| `source_config.seller_policy_prior` | `D` group 3 | Row creation precedes scoring; first score reads it | **L5** (C6, `TimeStamped.updated_at`) | Operator action; the prior falls back to the S2-12 default and needs `score_refresh --force` (§6) | **L5** (C6) |
| `cohort_baseline` current version | `D` group 4 | **L1** (C2) on promotion — including promotion **to and from NULL**, the §3.3.5(i) baseline-void transitions, which is why L1 compares with `IS DISTINCT FROM` (revision 8, SA-002) | Versions are immutable (§3.3.5a) — there is no update to detect; the mutable detection stamps revision 6 kept here live on the pointer since revision 7 (§3.3.5b, SA-025), where every evaluation rewrites them | Prune deletes only **uncited** versions; `PROTECT` makes a cited deletion impossible (§3.3.5f) | Restoring an older version row does not repoint the pointer; the pointer is what L1 reads |
| Cohort membership as a whole | `cohort_baseline` | **B5** (union rule) | **B5** | **B5** — an emptied cohort compares against the empty projection | **B5** |
| `algorithm_version` and the §3.4.3 constant set | everything | **B6 / L7** on release | **B6 / L7** — a constant change *is* a version bump (S2-6) | Retirement is gated on zero citations (§3.4.3 item 5) | A restored older deployment reports an older `ALGORITHM_VERSION`: **B6/L7** fires in the direction of the running code |
| `listing` membership in `P` | scope of everything | **L6** (C1) on entry | `mark_relisted` / `expires_at` push: **L6** | Leaving `P` is **not** a rescore — §2.3(b) clears, deliberately | Re-entry is an entry: **L6** |
| **Seller fan-out membership** — *which* listings a `seller_rating_observation` deletion must reach (revision 11, review finding SA-009 of `b2eedc33`) | L9's clear set; §3.4.2's seller anchor | A listing entering `P` adds a member with **no `Listing` or `Seller` write at all** (`acquisition/persist.py:74-102`), so no stamp moves and **no trigger is possible** — and none is needed, in two independent halves: a new member has no current score, so there is nothing for L9 to clear; and it cannot acquire one from the rating being deleted, because §3.4.2 makes its refresh take the **`seller` anchor** first. This is the row the round-4 finding existed to force into the table | Seller reassignment moves a listing between fan-outs: **L2 (C3)** in **both** directions, because `seller` is in `SCORING_INPUT_FIELDS` (§2.1) — into the swept seller (the phantom the anchor blocks, then C3 rescores) and out of it (the listing L9's query misses, then C3 repairs) | A listing leaving `P` leaves the fan-out: **not a rescore** — §2.3(b) clears its `current_*` unconditionally on delist or redaction, so a score citing a deleted rating cannot survive there | An operator import or replay that attaches a listing to a seller is still a `Listing` write: **L2 (C3)**. Deleting a `Seller` row is `SET_NULL` on `Listing.seller` (`catalog/models/market.py:241-243`) and bypasses `Listing.save()`, so no stamp moves — it joins the **operator-only** exceptions named below and in §6, with the same `score_refresh --force-listing` remedy |
| `OfferSnapshot.ingested_at` itself | B1 only | Server-assigned (§2.2.2) | Never written after insert | Dies with its row | Migration backfill sets it from `observed_at`, flagged; B3 remains the backstop for the migrated rows |

Two entries are deliberately **not** covered and both are named rather than hidden: an operator deleting a catalog or `SourceConfig` row out from under a live resolution, and an operator editing a delisted listing's stored attributes. Both are outside the automated write paths (nothing in `acquisition/`, `matching/` or `scoring/` performs them), both are recorded in §6, and both have the same remedy — the targeted `manage.py score_refresh --force-cohort <key>` / `--force-listing <id>` run, which recomputes without waiting for a trigger.

**Test obligation (MS-2d exit).** Each of B1–B6 and L1–L9 is exercised **in isolation, without adding an unrelated snapshot**, asserting that the next refresh updates exactly the affected baselines and listings and nothing else. B1 requires the §2.2.2 backfilled-insert case; B2 requires clock advancement past the **pointer's** `next_window_exit_at`, together with §3.3.5 test 5's assertion that a digest-identical evaluation advances it and clears B2 (SA-025); B3 requires a deletion that leaves `count(E)` unchanged; B4 requires a `SourceSite` rename with no listing row touched (SA-025); B5 requires a same-grain re-resolution, an extracted-attribute move across cohorts (SA-023), **and** the provenance-only and source-re-attribution cases of §3.3.5 tests 6–7; L8 and L9 require running the retention sweep, **and both additionally require §3.4.2's barrier interleavings in each direction — an isolation case cannot see either race** (L8: revision 9, SA-006; L9: revision 10, SA-007, whose three fallback shapes — older usable row, unusable row, S2-12 prior — are three separate fixtures because §3.2.5(d)'s post-delete aggregate differs in kind across them); **C5 requires the §3.2.5 prior → first-observation cases in both usability shapes, which are the direction a bare `>` cannot detect (revision 11, SA-008), and L9 additionally requires §3.4.2's four phantom fixtures, in which the affected listing is deliberately outside the sweep's own lock set (SA-009); B2 additionally requires the §3.3.5(i) NULL-deadline case — a void cohort must not re-fire B2 on a quiet tick — and B3 additionally requires the union case where a cohort has a pointer row and no group row (SA-002); C9 requires all seven §2.2.1 cases, of which test 7 is the write-amplification guard (SA-001)**. **Each candidate clause C1–C9 is additionally asserted to be the clause that selected the listing**, so a trigger that fires for the wrong reason — or that would stop firing if another clause were removed — is caught rather than masked by an overlapping one.

**The SA-014 no-drift test, stated as an obligation rather than left implied (MS-2d, live DB):**

1. Seed a cohort whose oldest observation is well inside the 90-day window. Run a refresh; record `baseline_digest`, `computed_at`, `last_evaluated_at`, `n_eff`, `λ`, every member's `q`, and the `listing_score` row count.
2. **Advance the clock by one hour without crossing any window boundary** and refresh again. Assert: `n_eff`, `λ` and every `q` are **bit-identical** to step 1 after the 6-dp quantization; the recomputed `baseline_digest` equals the stored one, so **zero new `cohort_baseline` version rows** are inserted and the pointer still names the step-1 version; `current_version.computed_at` is unchanged; `cohort_baseline_current.last_evaluated_at` has advanced and the **only** writes anywhere are that one pointer row's evaluation stamps (§3.3.5b: `last_evaluated_at`, `ingest_high_water` and the detection stamps, none of whose values may differ in this fixture because nothing was polled); **zero** new `listing_score` rows; and every `listing.current_scored_at` is untouched.
3. **Advance the clock past the oldest observation's 90-day boundary** and refresh again. Assert B2 fires, the new version's observation vector loses exactly that element, **a new `cohort_baseline` version is inserted** with a different `baseline_digest` and its own `computed_at`, the pointer is promoted to it in the same transaction, the step-1 version still exists **unmodified**, and exactly the cohort's members are rescored.

Step 2 is the regression guard for this finding: if a later change reintroduces a time-dependent statistic, it goes red rather than quietly resuming hourly write growth.

**L2 provenance test (SA-003, MS-2d, live DB).** Rewrite one member's `Listing.extracted_attrs_json` so that a single attribute's `confidence`, `layer`, or `source_text` changes while its `value` — and therefore every normalized input and the whole computed decision — stays identical.
