**the §3.2.6 / §3.4.1 neutral-payload tests — exact column values, exact group-4 payload and byte-identical explanation reproduction for all three neutral `price_basis` values (SA-027)**; **the §2.2.1 no-eligible-snapshot tests — the exact group-1 payload (price keys JSON `null`, `stock_status = "unknown"`), `s_avail = 0.50`, both risk flags, `offer_observed_at IS NULL` and its CHECK in both directions, plus the discriminating negative control that inserting another NULL-`fx_rate` snapshot with a different `stock_status` writes nothing (SA-005 of audit `b2eedc33`)**; **the §3.4.2 serialization tests — eleven two-connection barrier cases: six on the offer half (both refresh/sweep interleavings, the trailing-`offer_observed_at` repoll, the non-selected-snapshot and `price_unavailable` negative controls, the deadlock control — SA-006 of audit `b2eedc33`) five on the seller-deletion half (both orderings against all three §3.2.5(d) fallback shapes — older usable row, unusable row, S2-12 prior — plus the non-selected-rating no-op and the population invariant that no current score cites a deleted rating; SA-007) and four on the phantom half (`P`-entry and seller-reassignment phantoms, reassignment *away* from the swept seller, and an unrelated-seller negative control proving the anchor is seller-scoped rather than a global gate — SA-009), all of audit `b2eedc33`**; **the §3.2.5 first-observation tests — five listings quiet on the S2-12 no-row prior, then a first usable and a first unusable observation, each asserting C5 alone selects all five exactly once and that the following quiet tick writes nothing (SA-008 of audit `b2eedc33`)**; write-on-change idempotence and its explanation-only-change counterpart; the grain-upgrade and same-grain-retarget rescore paths.
- **Reproducibility (NFR-007):** a golden test that re-derives a stored historical score from its own `inputs_json` + `algorithm_version` **through `scoring/dispatch.py`, never through `compose.py` (§3.4.3 item 2, SA-010)** and asserts equality of the score **and of `explanation_json` / `explanation_text`, margin-in-IQR and source-composition clauses included (SA-015)** — run for **every** released version on every change, not only the current one. This is the single test that proves DR-004 and NFR-007 together, and it is the one that fails if any score-shaping constant escapes into a mutable row (S2-6) or if a shared helper change moves a historical output. **It runs with no access to `offer_snapshot` or to any `cohort_baseline` row**, which is what makes it a genuine reproduction rather than a recomputation: §3.4.1 group 4 carries the sufficient cohort evidence (§3.3.5d, SA-015), and `v<N>` parses `inputs_json` with its own version-local parser (§3.4.3 item 1, SA-010).
- **Version immutability (§3.4.3, strengthened again per round 3's SA-010):** the `MANIFEST.json` **directory**-digest check in `scripts.check` compared against the merge-base manifest; the **`registry_digest`** check that puts `versions/__init__.py` inside every entry; and the import-boundary check that no `scoring/versions/v<N>/` module imports *any* shared module, `scoring/contracts.py` included. Negative controls, all of which must go red: edit a released module alone; edit it *together with* its manifest entry; add an import of `scoring/stats.py` and separately of `scoring/contracts.py` inside a released package; repoint or remove a registry key with no file changed; delete a released package without an allowlist entry. Two must go **green** and are equally load-bearing: adding a new version with every existing `registry_digest` updated and no directory digest touched; and changing a `scoring/contracts.py` default while a released version's dispatched output stays byte-identical.
- **Retirement (§3.4.3 item 5):** the citation command refuses to run without an explicit `--database-url`, exits nonzero on a seeded citing row, and `scripts.check` makes no database connection at all — the gate cannot prove retirement and the test asserts it does not try.
- **Explanation payload:** snapshot-tested (syrupy) so an accidental payload-shape change is a visible diff, since MS-3 renders it.
- Gate (`uv run python -m scripts.check`) green at every commit; each sub-milestone PR runs CI + dependency review.

## 6. Risks / open edges

| Risk / open edge | Posture |
| --- | --- |
| **Every §1 decision was `pending` (audit `9f6079cb` SA-001, not audit `b2eedc33`’s SA-001, which is §3.4.1 candidate clause C9).** The MS-2a plan could not be cut until the owner disposed of S2-1…S2-26 | **Resolved 2026-09-06 by owner ratification** — the one way it could be closed, since this document never could. Revision 3 added **S2-17** (the §3.2.4 rubric constants), **S2-18** (the §3.3.4 observation vector) and **S2-19** (the §3.4.4 `ScoringRun` model); revision 4 added **S2-20** (append-only baseline versions), **S2-21** (the seller-feedback contract), **S2-22** (the current-offer selector) and **S2-23** (`is_provisional` semantics); revision 5 added **S2-24** (the `ingested_at` insertion stamp), **S2-25** (the left-censored price-event definition) and **S2-26** (the neutral-path baseline FK). All 27 rows are now `accept` (§1.2), the recommended default verbatim, **no override**, so no normative section changed and no OQ was opened. **The history is still the signal**: three consecutive review rounds each added ratifiable decisions, so the ratification debt grew while the document converged — and it was discharged in one disposition rather than being reduced by argument |
| **Audit `9f6079cb` closed at its five-round cap, not at convergence.** SA-025, SA-026, SA-027 and the reopened SA-019 were fixed in **revision 7, after the last peer round**, so no peer round saw those four fixes | **Superseded by the owner's choice**: rather than accept in-house verification alone, the owner opened a **fresh peer audit against revision 7** — the branch this row and §7 named. The four revision-7 fixes have now been peer-read as part of that audit's round 1, which raised no finding against any of them |
| **Audit `b2eedc33-99bf-4c33-bbfb-bb7b2bed7cd3` closed at its five-round cap on 2026-09-06 without converging.** Round 1 returned four findings (SA-001, SA-002 blocking; SA-003, SA-004 medium), all closed in revision 8. **Round 2** confirmed those four resolved and returned two blocking findings — SA-005 and SA-006 — both closed in revision 9. **Round 3** confirmed all six resolved and returned one blocking finding, SA-007 (the refresh revalidated the offer selector but not the concurrently deleted seller rating), closed in revision 10. **Round 4** confirmed SA-007 resolved and returned two blocking findings — SA-008 and SA-009 — both addressed in revision 11. **Round 5, the last round the audit had, confirmed SA-001…SA-008 resolved and returned SA-009 as `partially_resolved`**: the seller-anchor *mechanism* was accepted, and the residual was two normative sentences — the MS-2a deliverable bullet and the §3.4.1 coverage row — still describing the superseded listing-only lock. **That residual is closed in revision 12 with in-house verification only**, since the audit is over and no peer round will read it | **Closed at its cap, not converged — and the owner disposed of that on 2026-09-06 by accepting the in-house closure of the SA-009 wording residual**, so the MS-2a plan precondition is **met** and the plan is being cut (§7, `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-06-ms2a-scoring-substrate.md`). The acceptance is a decision about *this* residual on its own facts — a documentation inconsistency against a mechanism the same round had read and accepted — and it does not retroactively convert either audit's cap into convergence. **Revision 12's own fix has had no peer round**, and that stands as recorded rather than being softened by the disposition. **The pattern across four rounds is the thing worth recording, because it is what round 5 should be pointed at:** round 1 exposed structural completeness gaps, and every round since has found a boundary **opened by the previous round's own fix** — SA-005 by making the `price_unavailable` path reachable, SA-006 by introducing the mutable `current_offer_observed_at` stamp, SA-007 by stating a commit-time contract narrowly enough that a second deletion dependency fell outside it, and SA-008/SA-009 by the seller evidence that fix pulled into scope. **Two of the four fixes were generalizations of the mechanism rather than repairs of the case**, which is the deliberate response to that pattern: revision 10 made the contract a property of `D` (`D_selected`) with enumerated membership, and revision 11 made the lock an **anchor** chosen by whether an affected set is fixed or predicate-shaped, with the dependency × mutation pass re-run against fan-out membership. **Two claims were withdrawn rather than patched** — revision 9–10's one-directional wait-graph proof, which the second lock class invalidated, and "no score is ever written from evidence that will be deleted", which is unachievable and is replaced by the provable serialization statement. **The round-5 residual is itself the pattern's last instance and is worth recording as the lesson**: four rounds hardened a mechanism, and the thing that survived to the final round was not the mechanism but **two sentences elsewhere in the document that still described the version it replaced**. Both were in *plan-facing* text — a deliverable bullet and a coverage cell — which is exactly where an inconsistency is most expensive, because a plan may read its own sub-milestone contract and never reach the detailed section that corrects it. Residual risk is therefore of two kinds: a third predicate-shaped affected set in a dependency still treated as fixed (mitigated by §3.4.2 and the coverage table now stating, per term, which shape it is and what covers it), and **further plan-facing text that a late revision updated only at its detailed site** — for which the mitigation is the MS-2a and MS-2d exit criteria added in revision 12, which now name the anchor, the lock order and the fifteen barrier fixtures where a plan will actually look |
| **Revision 9's serialization contract makes the retention sweep wait on the score refresh** (§3.4.2, SA-006 of `b2eedc33`) | Deliberate, and it is the cost of not minting a score row from evidence that is being deleted. The wait is bounded by one refresh **write** transaction per listing — reads and scoring computation are outside the lock by construction — but that bound is a design property, not a measurement. **MS-2d must record the sweep's blocked time against the retention sweep's own budget**, the same obligation MS-2c carries for baseline growth. The named fallback if it is material is §3.4.2's rejected abandon-only protocol, whose residual is one S2-2a TTL of a just-deleted price surviving in an `inputs_json` — an eBay-boundary cost, so adopting it is an owner call, not one made here |
| **Revision 7 widens the baseline version identity** (the semantic `source_composition` joins `baseline_digest`, §3.4.1, SA-025) | Deliberate, and it is the branch the reviewer offered that keeps explanation evidence immutable rather than merely current. It adds an administratively rare minting term and removes two poll-cadence ones, so §3.3.5(h)'s order estimate is unchanged — but that estimate is still a derivation, and MS-2c's measurement obligation now covers the re-attribution case too |
| **The weighted-quartile convention deliberately differs from the `q` convention** (§3.3.4, SA-026): quartiles aggregate ties at full weight, `q` at half | The two answer different questions, and the only agreement that matters is proved rather than assumed — `q < 0.5 ⇒ y_i ≤ q50` and its mirror — so the rank clause and the margin clause of one explanation can never contradict each other. A reader who expects `qP` to invert `q` will be surprised; that expectation is precisely what was undefined for singleton, all-equal and tie-heavy cohorts |
| **Append-only baseline versions grow the database in a way revision 3's upsert did not** (§3.3.5h): order 10¹ new versions per cohort per day and order 10¹ MB/day at v1 scale — **now derived from a definition that produces it** (S2-25; revision 4's definition would have produced ~10³ MB/day, SA-021) | **Derived estimate, not a measurement** — the weakest number in §4. Bounded by content-addressed digests (a no-op refresh writes nothing), the §3.3.5(f) prune rule, and `BASELINE_VECTOR_MAX_ELEMENTS`. **MS-2c must measure it and MS-2d must not be enabled in production until the measurement is recorded** against §4 and CT-116. The named follow-up if the measurement exceeds budget is releasing the vector on old non-current versions, which costs the independent-audit path and is therefore an owner call, not one made here |
| **A version cited by a `merchant_fact` score is effectively permanent** — both for `cohort_baseline` versions (§3.3.5f) and for `scoring/versions/v<N>/` code (§3.4.3 item 5) | Intended, not a leak: the cited artifact *is* the evidence the score is audited against, and NFR-007 is the requirement being served. Only bounded eBay citations expire. The cost is bounded storage and one code directory per released version |
| **Version retirement cannot be proven in CI** (§3.4.3 item 5) | Stated as a limit rather than papered over. The proof runs against the production database and against the **whole retained backup set** (master-spec §18.6), reduced to the `t_oldest > t_last` date rule; `scripts.check` has access to none of it and must not be given it. The gate makes deletion impossible without an allowlist entry; the entry records that a human ran the proof, with the dataset identifiers, timestamps and counts |
| **A version is unretirable for up to six months after its last citation is deleted** (§3.4.3 item 5, revision 5) | The direct cost of doing SA-010 properly: §18.6 keeps 6 monthly dumps, so a dump that still cites the version stays restorable for that long. Accepted — a retained version directory costs kilobytes, and the alternative is a restore that produces unreproducible scores. The date rule makes the wait *checkable* instead of leaving it to judgement |
| **Operator-only mutation paths are outside the invalidation model** (§3.4.1 coverage table): deleting a catalog or `SourceConfig` row under a live resolution, or hand-editing a delisted listing's stored attributes | Named rather than papered over — no code path in `acquisition/`, `matching/` or `scoring/` performs either, so no trigger watches for them. The remedy is the targeted `manage.py score_refresh --force-cohort <key>` / `--force-listing <id>` run, and the runbook step is to use it after any manual catalog surgery. Closing them automatically would mean watching catalog deletions with a trigger the schema does not have |
| **B3's grouped digest query is a 90-day scan on every tick** (§3.4.1C) | The price of making B3 a content comparison instead of a count (SA-020). Bounded by hypertable chunk exclusion and measured in MS-2d; the named tunable is `B3_INTERVAL` (proposed 5 min), which lags detection without losing it, because a digest mismatch does not expire. Weakening B1 is explicitly not the tunable |
| **A price unchanged for more than 90 days is weighted at its true age** (§3.3.4, S2-25), so long-stable listings contribute little weight | Deliberate, and the alternatives are worse: clamping the carry-in to the window start makes its weight move on every tick (write amplification), and anchoring the window to the newest observation stops evidence ageing out at all. `n_eff` is scale-invariant, so a cohort of uniformly old prices still reports its full effective size; only a *mixture* of ages is discounted, which is what decay means |
| **Neutral-path scores carry a NULL baseline FK** (§3.3.5c, S2-26) | Consumers must handle it: MS-3 reads `price_basis` before dereferencing the baseline, and the CHECK constraints make the pairing impossible to get wrong at write time. The alternative — a synthetic empty baseline version per cohort — would put a row that summarizes no evidence into the audit path and onto the prune path |
| **`ingested_at` on pre-existing rows is a reconstruction** (§2.2.2), not an observation | Marked as such by `ingest_stamp_backfilled` so an audit can tell them apart, and B3's digest is the backstop for exactly those rows. The `INGEST_HIGH_WATER_LAG` margin also means a just-committed snapshot can be detected one tick late — a bounded latency accepted against the unbounded alternative of skipping it |
| **`seller.feedbackScore` may be a net score rather than a total count** (§3.2.5a) | Unconfirmed from repository evidence — the only in-repo statement (`docs/research/principled-deal-score-for-hard-drive-listings.md:101`) describes the buyer-facing display, not the API field, and no cassette exercises it. §3.2.5 gives the contract under both readings, `count_basis` records which applied per observation, and the confirmation is an MS-2e plan-time gate alongside the return-terms one. The conservative reading (net-as-lower-bound on `n`) understates a good seller's evidence, so the failure mode is a false cap — flagged, and the reason the confirmation is a gate rather than an assumption |
| **`is_provisional` no longer carries warranty uncertainty** (§3.2.6, S2-23), so a consumer that read revision 3's marker as "anything unverified" sees a much smaller provisional population | Deliberate and it changes a reported number: the MS-2e provisional share becomes a cohort-strength figure rather than a constant 100%. Nothing downstream consumes it yet (MS-3 is unbuilt), so the correction is free now and would not be later |
| **The conservative eBay boundary (S2-2b) costs statistical power.** Excluding the likely highest-volume source shrinks `n_eff` everywhere, so relaxation and the provisional marker fire far more often | Accepted deliberately: the design refuses to infer permission for a derived eBay price model (master-spec 404, 710). S2-2b was ratified `accept` on 2026-09-06 with this cost stated; MS-2e still measures it, so a later revisit has a number in hand. A bounded eBay-inclusive aggregate is the named alternative |
| **eBay return terms may not be on the response the adapter consumes** (§3, MS-2e). The round-1 document asserted availability; the adapter parses none, and `item_summary/search` may not carry it | Surfaced as an MS-2e plan-time investigation with a defined fallback (`returns_policy = unknown`, flag only, no cap). Not planned on until confirmed against current eBay documentation and a live cassette |
| **The SMR veto is structurally model-grain-only** (§3.3.3): `smr_dm` exists only on `DriveSpec`, and title extraction can never produce it | Stated, not worked around. MS-2e reports the fraction of listings where the veto can actually fire. Closing the gap is a catalog-coverage problem, not a scoring one |
| **Spec hygiene — master-spec §C.4 lines 1255-1262 carry the mixed-unit formula** that produced audit `9f6079cb`’s SA-005 (not audit `b2eedc33`’s SA-005, which is §2.2.1's no-eligible-snapshot payload): `base` in [0,1] in the same block as caps written as `35` / `60` | **Fixed by the 2026-09-06 master-spec hygiene revision** (same session). §3.2.1 remains normative for MS-2 and matches `docs/research/drive-deal-scoring-model-test-results.md:261-270`, which uses `0.35` / `0.60`; that revision normalizes the master-spec block to the same scale, so the mixed-unit reading that produced SA-005 no longer exists in either document |
| **ADR hygiene — `adr-0011-composite-deal-score.md:61` calls `q` a "cohort-relative weighted **cheapness** percentile"** while the same ADR's formula applies `1−q`, and the ratification prototype (`drive-deal-scoring-model-test-results.md:214-241`) computes an **ascending price** rank. Read literally, the ADR's own wording inverts the price signal | **Fixed by the 2026-09-06 master-spec hygiene revision** (same session), which corrects the ADR sentence. §3.3.4 remains normative for MS-2 and states the convention once: `q` is the **ascending weighted price rank with half-weight ties**, low `q` = cheap, `1−q` = cheapness, and `s_price` is monotonically decreasing in `q` — matching the prototype term for term. Revision 2 carried the ADR's inverted wording forward into §3.2 and §3.3.2 and is corrected |
| **Spec hygiene — master-spec line 1200** requires `offer_snapshot` `$/TB` to divide by lot quantity, but `$/TB` is not and cannot be an `offer_snapshot` column (capacity is not row-local) | **Fixed by the 2026-09-06 master-spec hygiene revision** (same session), which moves the requirement off the `offer_snapshot` row. §3.2.2 already honors it on the `listing_score` row, and the two now agree |
| **Spec §9 names two homes for the score.** `docs/specs/hw-radar-master-spec.md:441` — the `offer_snapshot` row is "time-series price/stock/FX/score"; `:451` — the downstream scoring group is "(`cohort_baseline`, `seller_rating_observation`, `listing_score`)", and DR-004 is titled "`listing_score` / explanation payload" | **Fixed by the 2026-09-06 master-spec hygiene revision 0.15** (same session), which leaves §9 naming one home. S2-1 — now ratified — chooses `listing_score` on the DR-004 + §9-downstream-groups reading and states why the snapshot column cannot carry a rescore; the spec revision matches that choice rather than the design following the spec after the fact |
| **Spec §6 glossary named a *third* home for the score.** Revision 0.15 fixed §9 and Appendix C.4 and left the canonical glossary entry for `offer_snapshot` reading "price/stock/FX/score", so the source-of-truth definition still contradicted S2-1 and a plan could reasonably infer a score column on the snapshot row | **Fixed by master-spec revision 0.16** (`docs/specs/hw-radar-master-spec.md`, §6 glossary + revision row), which drops `/score` and states that scores live in `listing_score` with only a denormalized current pointer on `listing`. Raised as review finding **SA-004** by audit `b2eedc33`, round 1, and closed in this revision. The lesson is recorded rather than just the fix: a hygiene pass that corrects a claim must sweep **every** definition of the term, and the glossary is the one most likely to be missed because it is not where the requirement lives |
| **§9:447 lists `dollars_per_tb` among "stored generated columns for row-local economics", and C.4's input table sources `$/TB` from "`offer_snapshot` generated columns"** — but capacity lives on `drive_spec`, so `$/TB` is not row-local and cannot be a generated column there. No such column exists today | **Fixed by the 2026-09-06 master-spec hygiene revision** (same session), which stops listing `dollars_per_tb` as a row-local generated column. §2.1 computes `$/TB` in the scoring library and persists it on `listing_score`; the only generated column MS-2 adds is `usd_total_landed_price`, which *is* row-local |
| **`OfferSnapshot.usd_item_price` multiplies `item_price`, not `total_landed_price`**, so the existing USD column cannot serve FR-004's "fold known domestic shipping (+ tax where known) into `$/TB`" | Surfaced; fixed by the §2.1 `usd_total_landed_price` addition rather than by changing the existing column, which other read paths may rely on |
| **The C.4 "other" seller policy prior is 0.50 and the veto is `seller trust < 0.50`** — the default sits exactly on the cap boundary | Not a contradiction, a trap: a `<=` implementation would cap every unrated first-party merchant listing at 60. S2-12 pins the strict inequality and MS-2b carries the boundary test |
| **Rounding-mode divergence from the ratification prototype** (§3.2.1): the research prototype uses banker's rounding, MS-2 pins half-up | Deliberate and pinned by a fixture at the exact `.5` boundary. Reproducibility requires a stated mode; the interpreter default is not one |
| Below-80% model-grain coverage at MS-2 close | Expected, not fatal (S2-10). §3.3.3's provenance ladder and S2-7's neutral price subscore keep every listing in `P` scored; MS-2e reports the shortfall to the backfill queue. C.3.5: shortfall signals catalog/rule gaps, never a reason to loosen precision |
| ADR 0019 is still `proposed` (`adr-0019…md:46`) when MS-2 starts | MS-2 does not wait (S2-10). If ratification later forces a `matcher_version` bump and a re-resolution sweep, the affected listings rescore through §3.4.1's L3/B5 triggers — no MS-2 rework |
| Cohort fragmentation from free-text `interface` / `form_factor` | §2.1 canonicalizes in the pure library rather than migrating the columns, and MS-2c **measures** real fragmentation. If measured fragmentation is material, a follow-up enum migration mirrors the `market_tier` change; recorded as a plan-time decision, not pre-committed |
| `CAPACITY_BUCKETS_TB` is generated from the seeded catalog (§3.3.1), so a catalog gap produces ad-hoc buckets | By design: an ad-hoc bucket is flagged and visible rather than silently merged. If ad-hoc buckets are common at MS-2c, the catalog seed is the defect, not the bucketing |
| The `verification_event` warranty lookup is unscheduled (C.4), so fitness `W` runs on **claimed** warranty for all of MS-2 | Accepted and marked with the `warranty_unverified` **risk flag** in every payload — **not** with `is_provisional`, which §3.2.6 reserves for cohort strength (SA-019). Scheduling the lookup is a spec-level change (§7/§19), not an MS-2 decision |
| `preorder` / `unknown` availability values are not fixed by ADR 0011 | **Now selected** in §3.2.4 at 0.30 and 0.50 (review finding SA-011) and put to the owner as part of S2-17. Constants under S2-6, with the ADR-fixed endpoints untouched; owner-tunable by a one-cell change and a version bump |
| **The seed rubric's `backorder_7d → 0.60` has no current `StockStatus` counterpart** (§3.2.4), so a merchant that distinguishes backorder from out-of-stock is currently flattened into one of the four existing values | Recorded as unmapped rather than silently approximated. Adding a `backorder` value to `StockStatus` is a schema change outside MS-2; §3.2.4 reserves 0.60 as its proposed seat so the addition is a version bump and not a fresh calibration argument |
| **The `W` rubric runs on *claimed* warranty for all of MS-2** (§3.2.4), because `verification_event` is unscheduled, and the modal cell is `unknown` channel × NULL months = 0.25 | Deliberate and visible: every MS-2 row carries the `warranty_unverified` **risk flag**, so fitness is dominated by `T` and `C` until warranty data exists — a stated consequence rather than a silent one. Revision 2's "stubbed at the unverified tier" wording did not say what the tier evaluated to; §3.2.4 now does. Revision 3's "contributes to the provisional marker" made every MS-2 score provisional and is withdrawn (SA-019, §3.2.6) |
| **An eBay listing is scored against a baseline it is not a member of** (§3.3.4), so the cheapest non-eBay listing gets `q = 0.5·w_self/Σw` while a cheapest eBay listing gets `q = 0` exactly | A direct consequence of S2-2b, bounded by half the listing's own weight share and shrinking as the cohort grows. Recorded in the explanation payload as `in_baseline`, so the asymmetry is visible to MS-3 rather than inferred from a score gap. It disappears entirely if the owner later reverses S2-2b |
| A refresh that exceeds its 60 s interval | `coalesce=True` + `max_instances=1` make this degrade to a longer effective interval, not to overlap. MS-2d measures and records the real runtime; if it approaches the interval, the interval — not the design — is the tunable |
| `docs/open-questions.md` lists **one open question, OQ22** — the retention class and `expires_at` policy for resolver-learned (`listing_derived`) `ProductAlias` rows, raised by the migration-0016 retention-index follow-up and **unrelated to MS-2** (the next question opened there takes **OQ23**). This document opens none | Every §1 item was a *proposed decision* inside MS-2's own boundary, not a spec-level open question, and all 27 were ratified `accept` on 2026-09-06 — an OQ would have been opened only by an override whose consequence reached spec text, and there was none. The spec-hygiene rows above are editorial corrections, not questions, and are now fixed. Revision 5 recorded "none open, next is OQ22", which OQ22's arrival made stale; the correction is recorded here rather than left to a reader to notice |

## 7. Execution process

Each sub-milestone: a plan document under `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-…-ms2<x>-….md` → execution with the full verification gate → a `dev→main` PR (merge commit, CI + dependency-review green). Deviations from this design or the spec go to the spec Deviations Log / OQ process, per Appendix B.

**Before the MS-2a plan is cut**, the owner disposes of every row in §1.2 (S2-1…S2-26) per §1.1. A rejected decision changes that sub-milestone's plan; it does not change this decomposition unless it moves work across a sub-milestone boundary, in which case this document is revised first.

**This happened on 2026-09-06**: the owner ratified all 27 rows `accept`, no override, so no sub-milestone plan is altered and this decomposition stands as written. The remaining precondition on cutting the MS-2a plan is therefore not ratification but the disposition of the cross-agent design review.

**Where that review stands, as of revision 12: audit `b2eedc33` is closed at its five-round cap, the owner has disposed of the residual, and the MS-2a plan is being cut.** The first audit (`9f6079cb`) reached its five-round cap on 2026-09-06 without converging: round 5 returned three open blocking findings (SA-025, SA-026, SA-027) and reopened SA-019, and revision 7 closed all four **after** the last peer round. §6 recorded the resulting choice — accept in-house adversarial verification, or open a fresh peer audit — as the owner's. **The owner chose the fresh audit.** Audit **`b2eedc33-99bf-4c33-bbfb-bb7b2bed7cd3`** was opened against revision 7; its **round 1** returned `revision_required` with four findings (SA-001 and SA-002 blocking, SA-003 and SA-004 medium), all closed in revision 8. **Round 2** ran against revision 8, confirmed those four resolved, and returned `revision_required` with two new blocking findings — **SA-005** (no defined stock fact for the mandatory availability subscore on the no-eligible-snapshot path) and **SA-006** (the score refresh can overwrite an L8 invalidation with a decision derived from a concurrently deleted snapshot) — both closed in revision 9. **Round 3** ran against revision 9, confirmed all six resolved, and returned `revision_required` with one blocking finding — **SA-007** (the refresh's commit-time revalidation covered the offer selector but not the concurrently deleted seller rating, so L9's one-time clear could be overwritten) — closed in revision 10. **Round 4** ran against revision 10, confirmed SA-007 resolved, and returned `revision_required` with two new blocking findings — **SA-008** (C5 is not total for the S2-12-prior → first-observation transition, because its comparand is NULL) and **SA-009** (the seller-rating deletion's lock fan-out is derived from the mutable population `P`, so a listing entering `P` or reassigned into the seller after lock-set selection can still be scored from the in-flight deleted rating) — both addressed in revision 11. **Round 5 ran against revision 11 and was the last round this audit had.** It confirmed **SA-001…SA-008 resolved** and returned **SA-009 `partially_resolved`**: the seller-anchor mechanism was accepted and the residual was a **wording inconsistency** — the MS-2a deliverable bullet and the §3.4.1 coverage row still described the superseded listing-only lock, so the implementation contract and the acceptance contract pointed in opposite directions. **This revision 12 closes that residual, and it does so with in-house verification only**, exactly as revision 7 did when audit `9f6079cb` hit its own cap.

**The precondition was an owner decision, and on 2026-09-06 the owner made it: *"accept the in-house closure."*** Both audits ended at their five-round caps rather than at a converged verdict, so this document could not supply the verdict itself. **The precondition is therefore met and the MS-2a plan is being cut** — `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-06-ms2a-scoring-substrate.md`, which goes to cross-agent review as a plan next, under the §7 process every sub-milestone follows.

**What the acceptance covers, stated narrowly so a later reader does not over-read it.** It disposes of **one** residual: SA-009's round-5 `partially_resolved` verdict, whose substance was two normative sentences contradicting a mechanism the same round had read and accepted. It rests on facts that are checkable rather than on a judgement about the audit as a whole — the fix changes no normative behaviour (no mechanism in §3.4.2 changed; one justification sentence there was made exact), and revision 12 additionally hardened the plan-facing surface where the inconsistency lived. It is **not** a finding of convergence for audit `b2eedc33`, and it does not retire the residual risks §6 records.

**The one cost carried forward, unsoftened.** No peer has read the reconciled text, and the failure mode both audits kept finding — a fix stated at its detailed site and not at its plan-facing one — is the class revision 12 closed by assertion. The mitigation is where the check now naturally lands: the MS-2a plan's own cross-agent review traces `lock_parents_for_delete` against §3.4.2, which is the round-5 reviewer's own `suggested_validation`, performed where the code is actually specified rather than in a further pass over the design. §3's MS-2a exit criteria carry that trace as an acceptance obligation, so it is a gate rather than an intention.
